115 年 8 月海關進出口貿易初步統計 
一、進出口貿易變動 
（一）出口 
8 月出口 824.0 億美元，創歷年單月新高，較上月增 9.4%，較上年
同月增 41.0%(按新臺幣計算增 52.6%)；累計 1 至 8 月出口 5,743.4
億美元，較上年同期增 44.2%(按新臺幣計算增 46.5%)。 
（二）進口 
8 月進口 601.0 億美元，為歷年單月第 3 高，較上月增 3.4%，較上
年同月增 44.3%(按新臺幣計算增 56.2%)；累 計1 至 8 月進口 4,374.3
億美元，較上年同期增 40.4%(按新臺幣計算增 42.4%)。 
（三）出入超 
8 月出超 223.0 億美元，為單月新高，較上年同月增 55.2 億美元；
累計 1 至 8 月出超 1,369.1 億美元，較上年同期增 500.5 億美元。

### Page 2

二、出口主要貨品 
（一）與上年同月比較 
受惠於人工智慧(AI)、 高效能運算及雲端服務等應用持續擴增，相關
產品出貨維持強勁， 加上價格攀升效 應，8月出口較上年同月增 239.7
億美元(+41.0%)，主要變動如下： 
1. 電子零組件出口 322.2 億美元(歷年單月新高)， 較上年同月增118.3
億美元(+58.0%)，其中積體電路增 115.5 億美元(+60.0%)、印刷電
路增 1.4 億美元(+29.9%)、 電容器及電阻器增0.7 億美元(+24.9%)。 
2. 資通與視聽產品出口 331.0 億美元，較上年同月增 97.6 億美元
(+41.8%)，其中電腦及其附屬單元增68.7 億美元(+40.4%)、儲存媒
體增 19.1 億美元(+2.4 倍)、 交換器及路由器增9.7 億美元(+1.1 倍)。 
3. 礦產品出口 20.0 億美元，較上年同月增 8.3 億美元(+70.2%)，其
中石油煉製品增 7.8 億美元(+74.3%)。 
4. 基本金屬及其製品出口 29.6 億美元，較上年同月增 5.4 億美元
(+22.4%)，其中銅及其製品增 6.1 億美元(+1.5 倍)，鋼鐵及其製品
減 1.1 億美元(-8.7%)。 
5. 機械出口 26.8 億美元，較上年同月增 3.6 億美元(+15.2%)，其中
生產半導體等機械增 1.9 億美元(+42.1%)、未列名具特殊功能機械
增 0.4 億美元(+39.0%)。

累計 1 至 8 月出口較上年同期增 1,759.8 億美元(+44.2%)，以資通與
視聽產品增 1,023.7 億美元(+68.5%)、電子零組件增 629.5 億美元
(+45.8%)增加較多。

生產等出現結構性轉變，爰另予併計。

三、進口主要貨品 
（一）與上年同月比較 
由於 AI 產業鏈國際分工與出口衍生需求之帶動，積體電路及資通產
品進口維持強勁，加上輸入物價明顯上漲，8 月總進口較上年同月增
184.5 億美元(+44.3%)，主要變動如下： 
1. 農工原料進口408.4 億美元， 較上年同月增129.4億美元(+46.4%)，
其中電子零組件增 82.5 億美元(+62.6%)、礦產品增 16.9 億美元
(+38.4%)、電腦之零附件增 14.6 億美元(+84.4%)、基本金屬增 4.5
億美元(+21.8%)、塑化原料增 4.0 億美元(+16.2%)。 
2. 資本設備進口 130.6 億美元， 較上年同月增 38.4 億美元(+41.6%)，
其中資通設備增 27.2 億美元(+1.2 倍)、半導體設備增 2.8 億美元
(+7.3%)、交通運輸設備增 2.8 億美元(+1.1 倍)。 
3. 消費品進口 55.7 億美元，較上年同月增 16.0 億美元(+40.5%)，其
中固態非揮發性儲存裝置增12.1 億美元(+3.7 倍)、藥粧用品增 1.2
億美元(+17.4%)、手機增 1.1 億美元(+78.1%)。

四、主要出口國家/地區 
（一）與上年同月比較 
8 月對陸港、日本出口創新高，對美國、東協為單月第 3 高 
1. 對美國出口 236.7 億美元，較上年同月增 40.6 億美元(+20.7%)，
其中資通與視聽產品增 23.0 億美元(+14.6%)、電子零組件增 11.6
億美元(+1.5 倍)、電機產品增 1.7 億美元(+30.1%)。 
2. 對中國大陸與香港出口 211.5 億美元，較上年同月增 59.7 億美元
(+39.3%)，其中電子零組件增 47.4 億美元(+45.6%)、資通與視聽
產品增 6.7 億美元(+42.9%)、 基本金屬及其製品增4.6 億美元(+1.2
倍)。 
3. 對東協出口 151.3 億美元，較上年同月增 44.9 億美元(+42.2%)，
其中電子零組件增 25.3 億美元(+50.3%)、資通與視聽產品增 12.6
億美元(+41.1%)、礦產品增 4.6 億美元(+1.5 倍)。 
4. 對歐洲出口 44.5 億美元，較上年同月增 11.5 億美元(+34.8%)，其
中資通與視聽產品增 6.6 億美元(+57.0%)、電子零組件增 4.0 億美
元(+1.1 倍)，基本金屬及其製品減 1.4 億美元(-31.0%)。 
5. 對日本出口 36.6 億美元，較上年同月增8.9 億美元(+32.0%)，其中
電子零組件增 8.9 億美元(+76.7%)、 化學品增0.5 億美元(+32.3%)，
資通與視聽產品減 0.6 億美元(-13.9%)。 
（二）與上年同期比較 
累計 1 至 8 月對美國出口 1,803.2 億美元，較上年同期增 631.6 億美
元(+53.9%)，對中國大陸與香港出口 1,394.7 億美元，增 307.5 億美
元(+28.3%)， 對東協出口1,150.7 億美元 ， 增372.3 億美元(+47.8%)，
對歐洲出口 376.6 億美元 ， 增130.7 億美元(+53.1%)， 對日本出口247.0
億美元，增 54.7 億美元(+28.4%)。

五、主要進口國家/地區 
（一）與上年同月比較 
8 月自中國大陸與香港進口 126.8 億美元 ， 較上年同月增47.6 億美元
(+60.2%)，自東協進口 106.9 億美元，增46.7 億美元(+77.6%)，自南
韓進口 91.7 億美元，增29.6 億美元(+47.7%)，自日本進口 57.0 億美
元，增 11.2 億美元(+24.4%)，自美國進口 53.9 億美元，增 14.3 億美
元(+35.9%)，自歐洲進口 49.5 億美元，增 6.2 億美元(+14.3%)。 
（二）與上年同期比較 
累計 1 至 8 月自主要國家/地區進口與上年同期比較 ， 自東協增75.4%、
自南韓增 63.2%、自中國大陸與香港增 40.5%、自美國增 24.4%、自
日本增 24.1%、自歐洲增 12.8%。

六、主要國家/地區貿易概況 
（一）今年 1-7 月主要國家/地區外貿景氣普遍上升，南韓(1-8 月)、香港、
新加坡出口動能尤為強勁，分別年增 52.8%、46.6%、38.3%；中國
大陸(1-8 月)、美國出口分別年增 19.3%及 14.6%，德國(1-6 月)、日
本各年增 10.9%、8.1%。 
（二）主要國家/地區進口同步上揚，累計 1-7 月以香港進口年增 48.1%最
為顯著， 新加坡、 中國大陸(1-8 月)分別年增 37.0%、27.0%，南韓(1-8
月)、德國(1-6 月)各增 18.7%、11.7%，日本、美國各增 6.5%、1.2%。

圖 5 2026 年主要國家/地區出進口年增率
七、綜合分析
（一）得益於人工智慧(AI)、高效能運算及 雲端服務等應用 需求強勁， 加
以外銷產品價格攀升，8 月出口 824.0 億美元，創歷年單月新高，年
增 41.0%，連續 34 個月正成長；進口 601.0 億美元，年增 44.3%，
主因 AI 產業鏈國際分工與出口衍生需求擴張 ，及農工原料行情上
漲；出、進口互抵後，出超 223.0 億美元，亦創單月新高 。累計 1
至 8 月出口年增 44.2%、進口增 40.4%。
（二）8 月出口主要貨品以電子零組件、資通與視聽產品貢獻最多，年增率
分別為 58.0%及 41.8%，前者受惠 AI 商機及記憶體外銷價量齊揚，規
模值創單月新高，兩類併計增 49.3%，其餘貨類平均增 16.3%，以礦
產品隨油品售價上漲，年增 70.2%較為突出，基本金屬及其製品、電
機產品各增 22.4%、22.3%。累計 1 至 8 月資通與視聽產品出口值已
超越歷年水準，與電子零組件合計占總出口比重達 78.7%。

（三）8 月對主要市場出口同呈雙位數成長，其中對中國大陸與香港、日本
受電子零組件外銷強勁推升，出口值同創單月新高，各年增 39.3%、
32.0%，對東協、歐洲、美國分別增 42.2%、34.8%、20.7%；五大市
場之外，對墨西哥及澳大利亞出口皆增逾 2 倍。累計 1 至 8 月對美
國、歐洲出口各年增 53.9%、53.1%，對東協年增 47.8%，對日本、
陸港各增 28.4%、28.3%，對五大市場出口規模均為歷年同期最佳。
（四）展望未來，地緣政治衝突 及美國關稅政策變化等不確定 因素，持續
干擾全球經濟成長力道，惟隨人工智慧應用 不斷深化，算力需求攀
升，國際雲端服務業者加速建置 AI 基礎設施，加以國內半導體及伺
服器等供應鏈具競爭優勢，均有助支撐我國外銷動能，下半年出口
可望維持成長態勢。

Document 076: 2027年春闘、初動のモメンタムは現時点で良好 ～労働側、経営側とも昨年からのトーンダウンはみられず～

Source PDF: all report pdf/2027年春闘、初動のモメンタムは現時点で良好 ～労働側、経営側とも昨年からのトーンダウンはみられず～.pdf
Report source: DLRI
Date: 2026-10-22
Pages: 5
Status: ok

### Page 1

Economic Trends

2026 年10 月 22 日

1/5 
2027 年春闘、初動のモメンタムは現時点で良好 
～労働側、経営側とも昨年からのトーンダウンはみられず～ 
 経済調査部 シニアエグゼクティブエコノミスト  新家 義貴 
（℡：050-5474-7490）

（要旨） 
○ 連合は2027 年春闘基本構想で、 定昇込みの賃上げ要求を 「６％中心、５％以上」 、ベア等の
賃上げ分を「４％中心、３％以上」とする目安を示した。前年よりも要求姿勢をやや強めて
おり、５％台の賃上げの定着に加え、さらなる引き上げを目指す姿勢がうかがえる。 
○ 経団連も高い賃上げの継続に前向きな姿勢を維持している。経労委報告の骨子案では賃上げ
モメンタムの「浸透・定着」が掲げられ、高い賃上げの継続に加え、中小企業への波及を重
視する姿勢が示されている。 
○ 日本経済研究センターのＥＳＰフォーキャスト調査によると、民間エコノミストは 2027 年
春闘の賃上げ率を4.96％と予想（厚生労働省ベース）。前年から小幅に鈍化するものの、高
い伸びが続く見通しであり、４年連続で５％台の賃上げを実現できるかが焦点となる。 
○ 春先にはイラン情勢悪化による賃上げ鈍化が懸念されていたが、現時点で労使双方の姿勢に
トーンダウンはみられない。企業業績の底堅さや構造的な人手不足が高い賃上げを支えてお
り、2027 年春闘の初動のモメンタムは良好と評価できる。

連合は、「６％中心、５％以上」と昨年からトーンをやや強める 
2027 年春闘の集中回答日は2027 年の３月中旬だが、実際の交渉に向けての動きは既に始まりつ
つある。 
本日、連合は「2027 春季生活闘争基本構想」を公表した。春闘に向けて最初に注目される重要
イベントであり、翌年の春闘に向けた労働組合側の基本理念や方針、重点課題を示すものであ
る。この基本構想をみることで、労働側が翌年の春闘にどの程度強気な姿勢で臨もうとしている
かを早い段階で確認することができ、春闘全体のトーンをつかむうえで重要な材料となる。 
今回の基本構想の最大の注目点は、定期昇給相当分を含めて「６％中心、５％以上」 、ベースア
ップ等の賃上げ分を「４％中心、３％以上」とし、より高い賃上げを目指す姿勢を示したことで
ある。「５％以上」という要求水準自体は４年連続（2023 年は「５％程度」）となるが、今回は
「６％中心」との表現が新たに加わった。ベア等の賃上げ分についても、従来の「３％以上」に

### Page 2

2/5 
「４％中心」が加えられている。前年の2026 年春闘では、全体で「５％以上」 、中小で「６％以
上」 、有期・短時間・契約等労働者では「７％」を目安とする、いわゆる「５・６・７」の要求が
示されていた。今回、全体についても６％を明確に意識する形となったことは、実質的には５％
を最低ラインとしつつ、より高い賃上げを促す姿勢を強めたものと受け取れる。単に高い賃上げ
を続けるだけでなく、その水準をさらに引き上げようとする意欲がうかがえる。

こうした姿勢の背景にあるのが、ここ数年の高い賃上げ実績である。2026 年春闘では連合ベー
スの賃上げ率の実績が5.01％となり、３年連続で５％台の高い賃上げが実現した。５％という水
準がかつてほど実現困難な目標ではなくなりつつあるなか、連合としても前年と同じ要求目安を
繰り返すだけでは、さらなる賃上げを促すメッセージとして十分ではないと判断した可能性があ
る。５％台の賃上げを４年目も維持するだけでなく、６％近辺を意識して賃上げ率のさらなる引
き上げを目指す段階に入ったというメッセージと位置付けられる。 
賃上げを取り巻く環境も、こうした強気の姿勢を支えている。構造的な人手不足が続くなか、
企業にとって人材の確保・定着は引き続き重要な経営課題であり、賃金引き上げの必要性は高
い。また、物価上昇率は2026 年度後半にかけて再び高まるとみられ、家計の生活コスト負担は重
い。名目賃金が増加しても、物価上昇に追いつかなければ生活水準の改善にはつながらない。実
質賃金を安定的にプラスにするためにも、労働側が高い賃上げを求める理由は十分にある。 
加えて、賃上げの広がりという点でも課題は残る。2026 年春闘では中小組合の賃上げ率が
4.69％と高い伸びを確保したものの、全体の5.01％には届かなかった。大企業と中小企業の間に
はなお差があり、正規雇用と非正規雇用の賃金格差も残っている。連合にとっては、高い賃上げ
春闘年 連合の要求目安（定昇相当分込み） 連合ベース 厚労省ベース
2020 ４％程度 1.90％ 2.00％
2021 ４％程度 1.78％ 1.86％
2022 ４％程度 2.07％ 2.20％

(出所）連合「春季生活闘争基本構想」、連合「春季生活闘争 第7回（最終）回答集計結果」、厚生労働省「民間主要企業春季賃上げ要求・妥結状況」
連合の賃上げ要求目安と春闘賃上げ率
実績

### Page 3

3/5 
を実現するだけでなく、それを幅広い企業や労働者に波及させることも引き続き重要な課題であ
る。今回の基本構想でも、賃金実態を把握できないなどの事情がある中小組合については
「20,000 円以上・６％以上」 、有期・短時間・契約等労働者については「７％」を目安とする方針
が示された。こうした点を踏まえれば、今回の基本構想は、高い賃上げの持続と一段の引き上
げ、そして賃上げの裾野の拡大を目指すものと評価できる。 
また、今回の基本構想では、新たに2030 年の賃金水準を2026 年比で15％程度引き上げる中期
目標も設定された。毎年の賃上げ率だけでなく、中期的に目指す賃金水準を明示することで、高
い賃上げを一過性のものに終わらせず、継続させようとする姿勢がうかがえる。 
なお、近年は個別組合の実際の賃上げ要求率がすでに６％近くに達しており、 「６％中心」とい
う今回の表現には、こうした実態を追認する意味合いもある。今回の基本構想からは、連合の強
い賃上げ意欲が確認できる一方、実際の賃上げ率が前年を上回るかどうかはまた別問題である。
今後は、主要産業別労働組合組織や個別組合の要求水準が前年からどの程度引き上げられるか
（られないか）が注目される。

経営側も賃上げの継続に前向き 
経営側も高い賃上げの継続に前向きな姿勢を維持している。経団連が毎年１月に公表する「経
営労働政策特別委員会報告（経労委報告） 」は、春闘に向けた経営側の基本スタンスを示すもので
あり、賃上げ交渉の方向性を占う上で重要な資料となる。2027 年春闘に向けた経労委報告につい
ては、現時点で原案は明らかになっていないものの、９月上旬には骨子案の内容が報道されてい
る。それによると、賃上げモメンタムの「浸透・定着」を掲げ、高い賃上げの継続と中小企業へ
の波及を促す方針とされる。 
経団連は2023 年を賃上げの「起点」 、2024 年を「加速」 、2025 年を「定着」 、2026 年を「さらな
る定着」の年と位置付けてきた。今回の「浸透・定着」という表現からは、高い賃上げを継続す
るとともに、その動きを中小企業などに広げることを重視する姿勢がうかがえる。また、経団連
の筒井会長は９月７日の記者会見で、 「ベースアップ実施の検討を引き続きスタンダードと位置付
けていきたい」と述べ、賃上げのモメンタムを維持する考えを示している。 
数か月前には、イラン情勢の悪化による原材料費や物流費の上昇が企業収益を圧迫し、2027 年
春闘では経営側が賃上げに慎重な姿勢を強めることも懸念されていた。しかし、現時点で経営側
の賃上げ姿勢にトーンダウンはみられない。 
なお、経労委報告では、原案から正式な報告にかけての修正は例年あまり見られないが、骨子
案から原案にかけては内容が多少修正されることもある。今後、10 月末～11 月中頃までに報道さ
れるとみられる経労委報告の原案が明らかになれば、経営側のスタンスをより具体的に判断でき
るだろう。

エコノミスト予想は4.96％、５％を巡る攻防か 
10 月14 日に日本経済研究センターが公表した「ＥＳＰフォーキャスト調査」では、民間エコノ

### Page 4

4/5 
ミスト 34 人を対象に2027 年春闘の賃上げ率について特別調査を実施している。これによると、
予測値の平均は4.96％となり、2026 年実績の5.18％を 0.22％ポイント下回る見通しとなった。
もっとも、前年から小幅に鈍化するとはいえ、ほぼ５％という歴史的に高い水準であり、2027 年
も高い賃上げが続くとの見方が中心である。なお、ここでの賃上げ率は厚生労働省「民間主要企
業春季賃上げ要求・妥結状況」ベースであり、連合の集計とは対象が異なる。 
予測の内訳をみると、定期昇給分が1.69％、ベースアップ分が3.27％となっており、ベアにつ
いても３％を超える伸びが見込まれている。また、予測値の高位８機関平均は5.23％、低位８機
関平均は4.68％となっている。予測には一定の幅があるものの、高い賃上げが続くという点では
概ね認識は共有されている。

2027 年春闘で賃上げ率が前年からやや鈍化すると予想される背景には、2026 年の物価上昇率が
2025 年より低く、物価高に対応するための賃上げ圧力が幾分弱まることがあると考えられる。ま
た、数年にわたる高い賃上げによって人件費負担が累積していることに加え、イラン情勢悪化を
背景とした原材料費や物流費の上昇も、企業の賃上げ余力を抑制する要因となり得る。一方、企
業業績が足元まで好調を維持していることや、構造的な人手不足と採用競争の激化は引き続き賃
上げを促す要因であり、賃上げ率が大きく鈍化する可能性も低いとみられる。 
ちなみに、昨年の同時期のＥＳＰフォーキャスト調査でも、2026 年春闘の賃上げ率は4.81％
（2025 年10 月調査）と、前年実績からの鈍化が予想されていた。しかし、実際には5.18％と予
想を上回り、３年連続で５％台の賃上げが実現した。今回も前年からの小幅な鈍化が見込まれて
いるが、予測平均は昨年の同時期をやや上回っている。イラン情勢の悪化という新たな懸念材料
があるなかでも、エコノミストの間では高い賃上げが続くとの見方が維持されていることが分か
（出所）日本経済研究センター「ESPフォーキャスト調査（2026年10月調査）」
（注）2027年は予測値、2026年までの実績は厚生労働省「民間主要企業春季賃上げ要求・妥結状況」

### Page 5

本資料は情報提供を目的として作成されたものであり、投資勧誘を目的としたものではありません。作成時点で、第一ライフ資産運用
経済研究所が信ずるに足ると判断した情報に基づき作成していますが、その正確性、完全性に対する責任は負いません。見通しは予告
なく変更されることがあります。また、記載された内容は、第一生命保険ないしはその関連会社の投資方針と常に整合的であるとは限
りません。 
5/5 
る。 
こうしたなか、予測平均の4.96％について、前年からの「鈍化」を強調するか、5％近傍という
「高水準の持続」を重視するかは、評価が分かれるところだろう。いずれにせよ、2027 年春闘で
は、３年連続で実現した５％台の賃上げを４年目も維持できるか、５％を巡る攻防が一つの焦点
となる。今後、経労委報告原案の判明により経営側の方針が明らかになるほか、主要産業別労働
組合組織の要求方針などが明らかになるにつれて、賃上げ率の見通しもより具体化していくこと
になるだろう。 
なお、この調査とは別に、毎年11 月～12 月にかけて、民間シンクタンク・調査機関から翌年の
春闘賃上げ率の予測が出されることが多い （筆者は 11 月に発表予定） 。 予測をレポートとして作成
することもあれば、 経済見通しレポートのなかで言及されることもあり、春闘賃上げ率の見通しに
ついて、この時期に市場の相場観が徐々に形成されていく。

2027 年春闘の初動は良好 
以上を踏まえると、2027 年春闘の「初動のモメンタム」は、現時点では良好と評価できる。イ
ラン情勢が悪化した数か月前には、原材料費や物流費の上昇による企業収益の圧迫を通じて、
2027 年春闘の賃上げ率が大きく鈍化するとの懸念もあった。しかし、ここまでの動きをみる限
り、労働側、経営側のいずれについても、前年と比べて賃上げ姿勢が慎重化している様子はみら
れない。連合が定昇込み、ベアともに前年より強い要求目安を打ち出し、さらに高い賃上げを志
向する一方、経団連も高い賃上げの継続と中小企業への波及に前向きな姿勢を示している。 
その背景には、原油などの代替調達が進み、資源の調達難が生産活動を大きく制約する事態が
回避されたことや、ＡＩ需要の拡大にも支えられ、企業業績が総じて堅調さを維持していること
があるとみられる。また、構造的な人手不足や採用競争の激化によって、企業が人材確保・定着
のために賃金を引き上げる必要性は依然として高い。物価上昇が続くなか、実質賃金の改善を求
める声が強いことも、高い賃上げに向けての動きを後押ししているとみられる。 
もっとも、原材料費や物流費の上昇による企業収益への悪影響が今後さらに広がる可能性はあ
り、先行きを楽観視することはできない。また、現時点で確認できるのは労使双方の基本的な姿
勢であり、具体的な要求や回答の水準はまだ明らかになっていない。今後は、経団連「経労委報
告」の原案や主要産業別労働組合組織の要求方針、早期に賃上げを表明する企業の動向などを通
じて、今回確認された前向きな姿勢が実際の賃上げにつながるかを見極める必要がある。

Kinsus

Solid revenue growth and margin 
improvement into 2027; Buy

Reiterate Rating: BUY | PO: 1,450 TWD | Price: 1,075 TWD

Raise PO along with hike in estimates  
We raise 2026/27/28E EPS by 4%/11%/14% after incorporating (see reports) 1) 
stronger-than-expected August numbers; and 2) strengthening growth outlook of server 
CPU (central processing unit) which is also shared by NYPCB. Our PO goes to NT$1,450 
(was NT$1,250) same based on 2H27-1H28E P/E. Reiterate Buy on Kinsus given its 
growing exposure to AI (artificial intelligence) server market via high-end ABF substrate, 
which in turn bolstering its margin profile in longer-term.  
Gross margin improvement carries into 2027 
We expect Kinsus’ GM (gross margin) to reach 27.8% in 3Q26, then followed by further 
improvement toward 29.3% along with 10% revenue QoQ in 4Q26, which will be fueled 
by continuous price hike and mix improvement. For 2027, expect the company’s GM to 
reach 32.5% driven by ongoing improvements in product mix and utilization.  
Sustained growth from AI CPU and optical module 
For AI and adjacent applications, we expect at least 15-20% revenue from substrate for 
Vera CPU in 2027, and AI-related application will account for 50% of the firm’s revenue 
in ABF (Ajinomoto build-up film) business in 2028. As for BT (bismaleimide triazine) 
substrate, growth in near/mid-term will be underpinned by the demand from DSP (digital 
signal processor) and transceiver (light engine) for pluggable optical module.   
Land acquisition for further capacity expansion  
Kinsus announced that it spent NT$6bn for acquiring land in Pingzhen, which will be 
used for K7 site expansion with capacity coming on stream in 2030 at earliest, following 
the ongoing expansion in K6 for ABF (Ajinomoto build-up film) substrate (reaching 80mn 
unit capacity/month in 2029-30).

Company Sector 
Semiconductors

Company Description 
Kinsus, founded in 2000, is headquartered in Taiwan and 
has production facilities in Taiwan (Taoyuan) and in China 
(Suzhou). The main business is IC substrate manufacturing, 
with a wide range of product offerings including SiP, FPGA, 
FCCSP, CSP, RF modules and FCBGA. Its clients include 
Qualcomm, AMD, NVIDIA, Broadcom, Xilinx, Altera, TI, and 
MTK etc. The main competitors include Unimicron (3037 
TT), NYPCB (8046 TT), Ibiden (4062 JP), Shinko (6967 JP), 
SEMCO (009150 KS) and AT&S (ATS AV), etc.

Investment Rationale 
We have an Buy rating on Kinsus, in view of its growing 
traction to AI server market from 2026 mainly via offering 
the ABF substrate for server CPU. For other businesses, we 
also recognize the solid growth outlook in BT substrate 
thanks to the demand from memory chips, while the 
growth in contact lens business should remain stable.

### Page 5

Price objective basis & risk 
Kinsus (KNSUF) 
We set our PO at NT$1,450 based on 35x 2H27-1H28E P/E, which is at mid-to-high-end 
level in its historical trading range (8-40x) since 2013. In our view, the multiple is backed 
by ROE and operating margin recovery to 36% and 25% in 2027-28E (vs 8% and 10% in 
2025-26E).

Upside risks to our PO are (1) tighter ABF supply, (2) increasing adoption of SiP and AiP, 
(3) lower competition pressure and (4) slow development of alternative technology.

Downside risks are (1) greater ABF expansion from peers, (2) muted SiP/AiP adoption, (3) 
more severe competition, and (4) unfavorable technology development.

M
Update
Delta Electronics Inc.  | Asia Pacific
Our Thoughts on USITC
Investigation No. 337
The near-term impact should be manageable, but we'll closely
monitor developments on this matter.
What's new:
• The US International Trade Commission (USITC) formally launched a patent
infringement investigation on October 9, 2026, following a complaint by
Vicor Corporation, a US-based power electronics company, against 20
entities, including Delta and others across the power supply, server assembly

and semiconductor space.
• Vicor alleges that certain imported vertical power delivery systems (used
mostly in HPC chips) have infringed US Patent 10,903,734.
• Vicor requested two types of remedy: 1) a Limited Exclusion Order (LEO);
and 2) Cease and Desist Orders (CDOs), which could restrict US imports and
sales of infringing products if granted.
Our view:
• We note that this is not the first time Delta and Vicor have had legal
disputes over patent infringement.

• We will closely monitor any developments in this matter, though we think
the impact on Delta's operations should be manageable (the products in
dispute account for high single digit % of its total revenue for 2026,  based
on our estimates).
• We remain OW.

Valuation Methodology and Risks
Delta Electronics Inc. (2308.TW)
Base case: P/E multiple of 30x based on average 2027-28 EPS.                          We believe our target multiple
is justified by the 63% earnings CAGR we estimate for 2025-28, implying a PEG of 0.5, versus
the stock's ~23x P/E and 23% earnings CAGR during 2022-25, which implied a PEG of 1.0.

n Faster-than-expected global economic growth

n Falling raw material prices
n Margin improvement
Risks to Downside
n Global economic slowdown
n Ongoing component supply tightness
n Rising raw material prices
n Margin contraction as a result of higher operating expenses or faster ASP erosion

Analyst Stock Ratings
Overweight (O). The stock's total return is expected to exceed the average total return of the analyst's industry (or industry team's) coverage universe, on a risk-adjusted basis, over the next
12-18 months.
Equal-weight (E). The stock's total return is expected to be in line with the average total return of the analyst's industry (or industry team's) coverage universe, on a risk-adjusted basis, over

the next 12-18 months.
Not-Rated (NR). Currently the analyst does not have adequate conviction about the stock's total return relative to the average total return of the analyst's industry (or industry team's) coverage
universe, on a risk-adjusted basis, over the next 12-18 months.
Underweight (U). The stock's total return is expected to be below the average total return of the analyst's industry (or industry team's) coverage universe, on a risk-adjusted basis, over the next

12-18 months.
Unless otherwise specified, the time frame for price targets included in Morgan Stanley Research is 12 to 18 months.
Analyst Industry Views
Attractive (A): The analyst expects the performance of his or her industry coverage universe over the next 12-18 months to be attractive vs. the relevant broad market benchmark, as indicated
below.
In-Line (I): The analyst expects the performance of his or her industry coverage universe over the next 12-18 months to be in line with the relevant broad market benchmark, as indicated below.

Cautious (C): The analyst views the performance of his or her industry coverage universe over the next 12-18 months with caution vs. the relevant broad market benchmark, as indicated below.
Benchmarks for each region are as follows: North America - S&P 500; Latin America - relevant MSCI country index or MSCI Latin America Index; Europe - MSCI Europe; Japan - TOPIX; Asia -
relevant MSCI country index or MSCI sub-regional index or MSCI AC Asia Pacific ex Japan Index.

Raising FAU estimates after upstream semi team's OE estimates changes: Our OE

shipment forecasts increase by 128%/52% to 17.73mn/73.87mn in 2027/28 (see full
report). As OE and FAU are 1:1, we raise our FAU volume forecasts accordingly, while
key other assumptions are unchanged, which resulted in an FAU TAM increase  from
US$778mn to US$1.773bn in 2027e and from US$4.86bn to US$7.387bn in 2028e.
This significant FAU TAM increase leads to the changes below.
We now value Largan at NT$9,040/share, or 36% upside potential: Our analysis

suggests that Largan's share price already embeds >30% global market share in FAU
before formal production starts in 2H27 or even 2028. But our revised assumptions
suggest further upside if Largan successfully ramps up production and gains
meaningful market share. To reflect 3Q earnings miss, we trim our intrinsic value
estimates from NT$3,000 to NT$2,800 per share for the company's traditional
business, while our TAM analysis derives a value of NT$6,240/share  for CPO/FAU

new business, bringing the price target to NT$9,040. Considering the stronger
growth potential in 2028, we upgrade the stock to OW from EW.
We now value Genius at NT$1,070/share, upgrade to EW: Similarly, we believe
Genius's share price is also affected by hopes for FAU components.  Using a similar
approach, we conclude that the aggregate value is NT$1,070/share (NT$600 for
existing business and NT$470/share for the FAU component business). We therefore

see a more balanced risk/reward and hence move to EW.
Key risks to our call: While upstream semi capacity and yield might have already
improved, we believe the yield of FAU coupling/matching will be a key overhang.
Any lower-than-expected yield could result in  shipment misses for FAU and OE. If
the yield is not ideal, key customers are less likely to place large orders despite
upstream capacity being ready. On the upside, earlier-than-expected mass

communications with a subject company, public appearances
and trading securities held by a research analyst account.
Key Takeaways
We trim Largan's traditional business value to NT$2,800/share but raise FAU
business to NT$6,240/share, from which we derive a new PT of NT$9,040.
We value Genius's traditional business at NT$600/share and FAU business at NT
$470/share, from which we derive a new PT of NT$1,070.
We are more positive on FAU's TAM but, we think FAU's coupling/matching could

be an overhang to our higher OE/FAU shipment estimates in 2027-28.

Executive Summary
Higher optical engine shipment assumptions as yields
improve
Our semi team has lifted their optical engine shipment assumptions following their latest
supply-chain checks. While PIC production remains a bottleneck, improving SoIC yields
and downstream assembly yields should support a faster OE ramp. They now expect OE
production to reach 2mn/59mn/148mn units in 2026/27/28, with actual OE shipments
reaching 0.39mn/17.73mn/73.87mn units, respectively. In particular, they assume the SoIC

yield improves from 50% in 2026 to 95% in 2027 onwards, while downstream assembly
yield rises from 20% in 2026 to 30%/50% in 2027/28. We believe this provides greater
confidence in the CPO volume ramp, although downstream assembly and testing remain
key gating factors for how quickly OE shipments can scale.

Largan: Larger CPO/FAU upside; upgrade to OW
Based on the semi team's assumption revision, we lift our CPO optical engine volume
assumptions for 2027-28e while keeping other assumptions unchanged:
1. We forecast CPO optical engine volume to increase from 0.39mn in 2026 to
17.73mn in 2027, with a further surge to 73.87mn in 2028. Consequently, FAU
(Fiber Array Units – vital optical interconnect components that align multiple

optical fibers with silicon chips, making them critical for Co-Packaged Optics)
volumes for CPO also grow from 0.39mn to 73.87mn in the same period;
2. We assume FAU ASP of US$100 in 2026-28 (average market level);
3. We forecast Largan's FAU market share to increase from 0% in 2026 to 5% in
2027 and 40% in 2028.
4. Assuming net margins of 10% in 2027 and 30% in 2028, we forecast FAU earnings
of US$9mn in 2027 and US$882mn in 2028.

use a more conservative multiple to reflect the first year of scalable production), this
implies an incremental market cap of US$30.862bn. Discounting this back to 2026 at a
10% rate, we derive a per share positive impact of NT$6,240.

3Q26 results miss; revising down traditional business estimates. Largan reported 3Q26

revenue of NT$15.65bn, down 11% YoY, with gross margin declining to 42.2% and net
income of NT$5.18bn, down 27% YoY. The weaker-than-expected gross margin, mainly due
to lower new-product yields and higher structural green-power costs, resulted in a
meaningful earnings miss. Together with softer smartphone order momentum, we lower
our 2026-28 revenue estimates by 5-6% and gross margin assumptions to
45.8%/44.5%/47.5%, resulting in 12%/16%/9% downward revisions to our 2026-28

earnings estimates, respectively. For more details, please refer to Earnings Estimate
Revision                  section.
We remain positive on Largan's CPO/FAU opportunity given the company's strong track
record in high-precision manufacturing relative to most Taiwan peers. Following our semi
team's higher optical engine shipment assumptions, we see a meaningfully larger FAU
addressable market in 2027-28. More recently, Largan indicated that its first FA project

has effectively passed customer verification and that it is expanding from FA into FAU,
including coupling and potential FAU-to-carrier assembly, with dedicated capacity
expected to support mass production from around mid-2027/2H27. While the current
share price already reflects some CPO/FAU expectations, our revised assumptions suggest
further upside if Largan successfully ramps production and gains meaningful market share.
We set our price target at NT$9,040, comprising NT$2,800 from the traditional business

and NT$6,240 from the CPO/FAU business.
Genius: Higher CPO/FAU optionality; upgrade to EW

Similar to Largan, following semi-team's higher assumptions, we also lift our CPO optical
engine volume assumptions for 2027-2028e while keeping other assumptions unchanged.
1. We apply the same FAU volume and ASP assumptions as we do for Largan.
2. We expect Genius to be involved in FAU components, rather than overall
solutions; therefore, we assume components TAM to be 15% of the FAU TAM.
3. We forecast Genius's market share to increase from 0% in 2026 to 5% in 2027 and

30% in 2028.
4. Assuming net margins of 10% in 2027 and 25% in 2028, we forecast FAU
component earnings of US$1mn in 2027 and US$81mn in 2028.
If we value the FAU component business at 25x 2028e P/E (lower multiple than Largan
because Genius has smaller scale), it could introduce incremental market cap of US
$2,004mn. Discounting it back to 2026 at a 10% discounted rate, we derive a per-share
positive impact of NT$470.

Similar to Largan, our residual income model derives an intrinsic value of NT$600/share
for the traditional business, while the TAM analysis suggests CPO/FAU-related
opportunities could be valued at NT$470/share. Adding these together, we raise our price
target materially to NT$1,070.
Given Largan's traditional business has faced margin headwinds in 3Q26 due to the
application of green power, it's reasonable to expect that Genius could also face similar

customer requirements on green power. However, as Genius has more production capacity
in mainland China, we believe it could face less significant cost pressure. We therefore
maintain our estimates unchanged at this stage and will monitor margin development in
the upcoming quarterly results.

Earnings Estimate Revision
Largan reported 3Q26 revenue of NT$15.65bn (-11% YoY) and net income of NT$5.18bn (-

results, softer smartphone order momentum and a lower gross margin outlook due to
new-product yield pressure and higher green-power costs, we lower our revenue and GPM
estimates for 2026-28, resulting in 9-16% downward earnings revisions over the period.

Valuation and Price Target Revisions
Largan: For the traditional business, we lower our earnings and medium-term growth rate;
as a result, our intrinsic value in our base case scenario is adjusted down to NT$2,800
from NT$3,000. We use RIM valuation methodology for Greater China Hardware Tech
companies in our coverage. We apply an unchanged CoE of 8.5%, a medium-term growth
rate of 8% (from 7% previously on higher 2026e estimates), and a long-term growth rate

of 3% (unchanged). However, we believe the upward revision to optical engine shipment
assumptions materially increases the addressable market and earnings potential for
Largan's FAU business. Therefore, we add the base case per share positive value from FAU
based on the analysis in the previous section. The new SOTP valuation derives a new price
target at NT$9,040 (from NT$7,100). We also raise our bull case and bear case values to
NT$11,426 (from NT$11,142.80)  and NT$4,620 (from NT$3,649.20).

Genius: For the traditional business, we make no earnings and intrinsic value revisions; as
a result, our intrinsic value under our base case scenario is unchanged at NT$600. We use
RIM valuation methodology for Greater China Hardware Tech companies in our coverage.
We apply a CoE of 8.5%, a medium-term growth rate of 4%, and a long-term growth rate
of 3%. However, we believe the upward revision to optical engine shipment assumptions
increases the potential value of Genius's FAU opportunity, although execution visibility

remains lower than for more established suppliers. Therefore, we add the base case per
share positive value from FAU based on the analysis in the previous section. The new
SOTP valuation derives a new price target at NT$1,070 (from NT$910). We also raise our
bull case and bear case values to NT$1,834 (from NT$1,705.20) and NT$658 (from NT
$592.40).

STOCK RATINGS
Morgan Stanley uses a relative rating system using terms such as Overweight, Equal-weight, Not-Rated or Underweight (see definitions below). Morgan Stanley does not assign ratings of Buy,
Hold or Sell to the stocks we cover. Overweight, Equal-weight, Not-Rated and Underweight are not the equivalent of buy, hold and sell.  Investors should carefully read the definitions of all
ratings used in Morgan Stanley Research. In addition, since Morgan Stanley Research contains more complete information concerning the analyst's views, investors should carefully read Morgan

Stanley Research, in its entirety, and not infer the contents from the rating alone.  In any case, ratings (or research) should not be used or relied upon as investment advice.  An investor's decision
to buy or sell a stock should depend on individual circumstances (such as the investor's existing holdings) and other considerations.

Analyst Stock Ratings
Overweight (O). The stock's total return is expected to exceed the average total return of the analyst's industry (or industry team's) coverage universe, on a risk-adjusted basis, over the next
12-18 months.
Equal-weight (E). The stock's total return is expected to be in line with the average total return of the analyst's industry (or industry team's) coverage universe, on a risk-adjusted basis, over

the next 12-18 months.
Not-Rated (NR). Currently the analyst does not have adequate conviction about the stock's total return relative to the average total return of the analyst's industry (or industry team's) coverage
universe, on a risk-adjusted basis, over the next 12-18 months.
Underweight (U). The stock's total return is expected to be below the average total return of the analyst's industry (or industry team's) coverage universe, on a risk-adjusted basis, over the next

12-18 months.
Unless otherwise specified, the time frame for price targets included in Morgan Stanley Research is 12 to 18 months.
Analyst Industry Views
Attractive (A): The analyst expects the performance of his or her industry coverage universe over the next 12-18 months to be attractive vs. the relevant broad market benchmark, as indicated
below.
In-Line (I): The analyst expects the performance of his or her industry coverage universe over the next 12-18 months to be in line with the relevant broad market benchmark, as indicated below.

Cautious (C): The analyst views the performance of his or her industry coverage universe over the next 12-18 months with caution vs. the relevant broad market benchmark, as indicated below.
Benchmarks for each region are as follows: North America - S&P 500; Latin America - relevant MSCI country index or MSCI Latin America Index; Europe - MSCI Europe; Japan - TOPIX; Asia -
relevant MSCI country index or MSCI sub-regional index or MSCI AC Asia Pacific ex Japan Index.

研 究  報 告                
貿聯-KY(3665.TT) 
產業：電子–其他電子 
正式合併 Interplex 財報，大幅挹注 HPC 產品營收 
 投資評價與建議 
正式併購Interplex，預估2026/2027年稅後EPS為70.58/128.86元，過往貿聯
本益比位於17~30倍區間，考量併購與光通訊專案逐步發酵，給予25倍PER。 
 重點評論分析 
正式併購Interplex集團Datacom業務，大幅挹注HPC營收：近期貿聯正式合
併Interplex集團資料通訊業務，並自2026年9月23日起納入合併報表。  
Interplex產品包含：伺服器機殼、機櫃、滑軌與CPU Socket等，併購有利提
升貿聯在單⼀機櫃之產值，另外有利切入過往較難打進供應鏈之CSP客戶。 
HPC和半導體設備仍為主要獲利引擎 ， 光通訊佈局逐步發酵 ：展望2026~2027
年 ， 營收動能主要來自 ： (1)Ethernet AEC規格逐漸從400G往800G迭代 ， 800G 
AEC主要採用客戶為xAI與AWS，1.6T AEC預計將於4Q26隨Nvidia Rubin新
平台開始正式出貨，ALC主動式光纜目前已有2個以上客戶表示願意採用，預
估2H27開始貢獻營收 ； (2)Vera Rubin機櫃功率突破300kW ， 帶動Power Whip
規格從60A提升至100A 、 機櫃內Busbar規格從1,400A提升至5,000Ａ ， 電源規
格提升帶動產品單價隨安培數增加而提升；(3)Rubin Ultra機櫃Power Rack設
計帶動Power Whip用量與ASP皆顯著增加 ， 預估產值成⻑2倍以上 ； (4)貿聯與
ams OSRAM合作開發microVCSEL互連方案，另外與SENKO和ficonTEC合作
全自動化光學互連與光纖製備流程，目前光通訊營收已佔整體營收約2~3%。 
 投資風險 
伺服器新平台出貨進度不如預期、原物料成本上漲。

### Page 2

2

| 營運概況  
2Q26毛利率重回成⻑軌道，HPC與半導體設備需求持續強勁，3Q26營收創高：2Q26營收季增11.6%至232.6億元，係
因HPC與半導體設備業務需求持續強勁，1Q26毛利率僅28.8%，主要受車用等非AI低毛利產品復甦導致產品組合改變
以及xAI因併購使其資料中心建置進度停滯 ， 導致延後拉貨AEC與power產品 ， 貿聯產能利用率下降亦影響毛利率 ， 2Q26
毛利率季增1.96ppts至30.7%，重回過往水準，係因客戶逐步恢復拉貨，稅後EPS為15.28元。貿聯8月營收月減7.7%至
88.9億元，係因歐洲地區歷經季節性暑期⻑假，9月營收月增5%至93.3億元，營收達成率101%，符合預期，係因xAI逐
步恢復拉貨以及AWS Trainium 3放量，3Q26營收季增19.8%至278.9億元，預估稅後EPS為19.12元。 
正式併購Interplex集團Datacom業務，大幅挹注HPC營收： 貿聯-KY於2026年6月宣布斥資8.5億美元收購新加坡
Interplex集團資料通訊業務，近期正式合併並自2026年9月23日起納入合併報表。Interplex營運總部位於新加坡，產能
遍及南亞與亞洲地區，包括：泰國、越南、⾺來⻄亞與中國等，其核心技術為精密金屬加工與機構件，主要產品包含：
伺服器機殼、機櫃、滑軌與CPU Socket等，另外僅Busbar產品線與貿聯重疊且同為Nvidia MGX合格供應商，併購除擴
張HPC營收規模外，亦提升貿聯在單⼀AI Server機櫃之產值，另外透過併購有利切入過往較難打進供應鏈之CSP客戶。
2025年Interplex營收約4億美元，佔貿聯2025年營收之17%，隨AI運算與推論需求增加，假設Interplex 2026和2027年
營收皆年增30%，預估可貢獻貿聯2026/2027年營收分別約39與200億元。 
HPC 和半導體設備仍為主要獲利引擎，光通訊佈局逐步發酵，成為「銅光並進」主要受惠者：展望 2026~2027 年，貿
聯營收動能主要來自：(1)隨 AI 頻寬需求增加，Ethernet AEC 規格逐漸從 400G 往 800G 迭代，預計 2026 年底前成為
主流規格，800G AEC 主要採用客戶為 xAI 與 AWS，其中 xAI 因 AEC 採用規格最高且⻑度最⻑，故 ASP 較高，1.6T 
AEC 預計將於 4Q26 隨 Nvidia Rubin 新平台開始正式出貨，由於 1.6T AEC 採用 8 條單通道 200G 之線材組裝，故組
裝難度提升且成本翻倍，因此 ASP 和毛利率皆優於過往規格，另外 PCIe AEC 則已開發完成，將隨 CSP 推出 PCIe 版本
Switch 放量，ALC 主動式光纜目前已有 2 個以上客戶表示願意採用，預估 2H27 開始貢獻營收；(2)隨 Vera Rubin 機櫃
功率突破 300kW，帶動 Power Whip 規格從 60A 提升至 100A、機櫃內 Busbar 規格從 1,400A 提升至 5,000Ａ，電源
規格提升帶動產品單價隨安培數增加而提升；(3)Rubin Ultra 機櫃可容納 576 顆 GPU 使單⼀機櫃功耗超過 500kW．由
於機櫃無法容納電源設備，故單獨拉出成為 Power Rack．帶動「Power Rack 至運算機櫃」電源連接需求大幅增加，預
估 Power Whip 之用量與 ASP 皆顯著增加 ， 預估產值成⻑超過 2 倍以上 ； (4)光通訊領域方面 ， 近期貿聯與 ams OSRAM
合作開發 AI scale-up microVCSEL 互連方案，另外持續與 SENKO 和 ficonTEC 合作全自動化的光學互連與光纖製備流
程，目前光通訊營收已佔公司整體營收約 2~3%，顯示光通訊產品成⻑⼒道強勁。綜上所述，預估 2026/2027 年營收
年增 49.5%/55.4%至 1,064.9/1,654.6 億元，稅後 EPS 為 70.58/128.86 元。

6805 富世達(FOSITEK)

壹、訪 談／法 說 內 容 
重點摘要： 
 2Q26 營收 37.4 億，YoY +42.8% ，毛 利 率34.05%，EPS 12.39
元。 1H26 毛利率  33.13% EPS 25.77 元。  
 隨著 VR200 與 ASIC 伺服器專案陸續放量 ， 伺服器產品比重提
升，再加上新產能第四季陸續開出，公司營收、毛利將維持成
長動能。  
 2027 年受惠於 Vera Rubin 平台與 ASIC 進入放量階段，下一
世代 Vera Rubin Ultra 機櫃，預期將導入更高規格 ZQD ，將
有望帶動單櫃快接頭產值提升。

一. 2Q26 毛利率達  34.05% ，EPS 12.39 元 
富世達 2Q26 營收 37.4 億， QoQ -10.6% ，YoY +42.8% ，營收較
前一季下滑主要由於手機、伺服器產品處於新舊產品轉換期影
響。而獲利方面， 2Q26 營業毛利 13.5 億， QoQ -5.8% ，YoY 
+101.6% ，毛利率 34.05% ，QoQ +1.74ppts ，YoY +9.98ppts ，
2Q26 營業利益 10.4 億，QoQ -6.9% ，YoY +109.9% ，公司表示，
受惠於製程持續優化、伺服器產品佔比提升至五成以上，帶動毛
利率提升 。2Q26 業外淨利 0.3 億 ， 稅後淨利8.49 億，QoQ -7.4% ，
YoY +157.3% ，單季稅後 EPS 12.39 元。

二. 預估 2026 年稅後 EPS 63.08  
公司公告 9 月營收 16.13 億， MoM +5.6% ，YoY +32.2% ，3Q26
營收 45.02 億，QoQ +36.1% ，YoY +79.3% ，今年累計營收 124.3
億， YoY +47.3% 。展望 2H26 ，公司預期隨著 VR200 與 ASIC 伺
服器專案陸續放量，水冷快接頭與滑軌的出貨比重將顯著提升，
再加上新產能第四季將陸續開出，公司營收將維持成長動能。而
摺疊手機部分，受惠於客戶發布新款產品，亦將推升摺疊手機業
務回溫。此外，由於高毛利伺服器產品比重提升，公司預期毛利
率將持續攀升，且營業費用率亦將隨營收規模擴大而逐漸下降。
預估 2026 全年營收 181.8 億元， YoY +46.5% ，毛利率 35.2% ，
營利率 29.4% ，稅後淨利 43.2 億，全年稅後 EPS 63.08 元。

三. 預估 2027 年稅後 EPS 112.24 元 
展望 2027 年，受惠於 Vera Rubin 平台與 ASIC 進入主要放量階
段， QD 應用亦從 Switch Tray 延伸至 Compute Tray 及 Rack 
Manifold ， 將帶動機櫃快接頭用量提升 。 此外 ， 由於晶片功耗提升 、
投資評等：買進

     26 X 2027 EPS

冷卻液流量需求增加， NVIDIA 下一世代的 Vera Rubin Ultra 機
櫃，預期將導入更高規格 ZQD ，單價預估將比現行的 MQD/UQD
顯著提升，將有望帶動單櫃快接頭產值提升。預估 2027 全年營收
295.5 億元， YoY +62.5% ，毛利率 37.6% ，營利率 32.3% ，稅後
淨利 76.9 億，全年稅後 EPS 112.24 元。

四. 投資建議為「買進」，目標價 2920 元 
考量到 AI 伺服器水冷滲透率持續提升， VR 及 ASIC 專案的強勁
需求，公司快接頭 (QD) 產品成長動能明確，公司受惠於各項專案
的陸續發酵，將顯著推升公司伺服器產品的營收貢獻，亦有望帶
動公司營收與獲利維持成長動能。目前股價交易於 2027 年 EPS
約 21~22 倍本益比，評價仍具上修空間，給於買進投資建議，目
標價 2920 元(26x 2027 EPS) 。

貳、產 業 及 營 運 概 況

一.公司簡介  
富世達 (FOSITEK) 成立於 2001 年，為奇鋐科技 (3017) 旗下子公
司，主要從事電子類、電腦、資訊通信產業等 3C 電子產品轉軸組
件之研發、製造及銷售。公司產品主要應用於電腦、電子、光學、
家電、資訊、通信、醫療設備等產品之連結軸，如商用 NB 轉軸、
工業用 NB 轉軸 、 軍事用NB 轉軸 、LCD Monitor 轉軸 、PDA 轉軸、
LCD-TV 轉軸、手機轉軸、螢幕懸臂支架、醫療懸臂支架等。公司
初期與京東方、華為三方合作生產研發摺疊手機，為華為摺疊手
機的主要開案合作廠商。近年積極投入水冷快接頭模組量產，並
也陸續送給鴻海、廣達等組裝廠進行測試，同時也通過數家水冷
板供應商認證，已成功打入輝達 GB200 與 GB300 供應鏈出貨水
冷快接頭 (QD/UQD) 與滑軌， 2025 年折疊手機佔公司整體營收的
56.4%， 筆記型電腦佔6.4%， 伺服器產品佔36.3%， 其他佔0.9%。
公司總部位於台北新莊新北產業園區，並於大陸深圳及越南設有
生產據點。公司 AI 伺服器與水冷零件主要客戶為 NVIDIA 及 CSP
大廠；手機主要客戶包含華為、 Motorola ，Google 等；筆電客戶
包含 Dell 、HP、聯想、華為。而公司主要競爭對手則包含新日興
(3376) 、兆利科技 (3548) 等。

二.產業趨勢概況  
1. 四大 CSP 上修今年資本支出達 7,450 億美元 
在生成式 AI 商機快速擴張帶動下，全球雲端基礎設施投資進入新
一輪高峰。亞馬遜、 Alphabet 和微軟認為其在先前的鉅額投資已
帶動營收明顯成長 ， 同時預估未來AI 潛在商機仍持續擴大 。 其中 ，
亞馬遜、 Alphabet 和 Meta 已陸續上調 2026 全年投資金額，且四
大雲端服務供應商 (CSP) 總資本支出將上升至 7,450 億美元新高
規模。亞馬遜、微軟、 Alphabet 與 Meta 2026 年第 1、2 季分別
為 1,306 億、1,701 億美元，同時亞馬遜、 Alphabet 和 Meta 再上
修 2026 全年資本支出金額， 2026 年資本支出預估將達 7,450 億
美元。而根據 TrendForce 最新產業研究，全球主要雲端服務供應
商(CSP) 為加速建置 AI 基礎設施，資本支出預估於 2026 年將年
增 98%，2027 年再成長 50% 至 1.3 兆美元左右，年增率收斂的主
要原因，除了反映高基期因素之外，部分可歸咎為鉅額資本支出
對四大 CSP 財務衝擊日益擴大。然而，在 AI Inference 、Agentic 
AI、自研 ASIC 和新一代 AI 模型持續推升算力需求的情況下，預
期整體 CSP 投資金額將續創新高紀錄，產業成長有望從原先以
GPU 擴張為主，轉為包含 AI server 、液冷散熱、先進封裝、高速
互聯、電源和記憶體等基礎設施全面升級的態勢。

2. 2026 年全球 AI 伺服器出貨量預計年增 31% 
根據 TrendForce 最新 AI server 產業研究，近期超大型 CSP 、
Tier-2 資料中心業者對 NVIDIA GB/VR 等整櫃式 AI server 方案的
採購意願明顯提高，且 Google 、AWS 新一代 ASIC 平台於 2026
下半年陸續放量，加上中系業者擴大採用本地 AI 方案以滿足雲端
大型語言模型 (LLM) AI 需求，促使 TrendForce 上調 2026 年 AI 
server 出貨年成長幅度，從原先的 28% 更新為近 31%。北美五大
業者 2026 年的資本支出多翻倍提升，反映出大型 CSP 廠持續擴
建 AI 資料中心、 GPU 叢集、液冷散熱等新一代基礎設施，以應對
生成式 AI 及大型模型快速成長帶來的算力需求。其中， Google 
2026 至 2027 年都將側重自家 TPU 晶片發展， 2027 年出貨量有
望繼續高速成長。 AWS 2026 年以 NVIDIA GB300 作為 GPU AI 
server 主力，自研 ASIC 也穩定出貨，預期至 2027 年量能將再擴
大。 Meta 2026 年主要採用 NVIDIA GB/VR 、AMD Helios 整櫃式
方案，自研 ASIC 布局預計至 2027 年將明顯加速，為該公司長期
降低 AI 基礎設施成本、提升推論效率的重要策略。

3.液冷散熱成高階 AI 基礎設施標配，估 2026 年滲透率達 53% 
根據 TrendForce 最新研究顯示，隨著 AI 基礎設施建置浪潮及高
階晶片運算功率不斷攀升，單晶片熱設計功耗已超越 1kW ，使液
冷散熱技術正式成為高階 AI 基礎設施的標準配置。在 NVIDIA 、
AMD 與 Google 等巨頭的高階晶片與整櫃式方案強力推動下，預
估 2026 年液冷散熱於 AI 晶片的滲透率將從 2025 年的 33% 大幅
躍升至 53%，並在 2027 年進一步逼近 60%。其中， Google 因積
極導入自研 TPU 並率先全面推廣整櫃式液冷設計，成為主要推手
之一。此外，隨著 NVIDIA Vera Rubin 平台全面導入全液冷架構，
水冷板、分歧管及冷卻分配系統等關鍵零組件需求隨之大增，帶

4. 蘋果入局帶動摺疊機高成長，估今年全球摺疊機出貨年增 24% 
根據 Counterpoint Research 報告 ， 全球摺疊智慧型手機累計出貨
量預計將於 2026 年底突破 1 億支大關，達成自 2019 年首度問世
以來的重大里程碑。隨著消費者對大尺寸書本式摺疊機的需求攀
升，加上蘋果 (Apple) 推出首款摺疊 iPhone 的帶動，預估 2026 年
全球摺疊機出貨量將年增 24% ，2027 年出貨預估年增 37% 。而
根據 Counterpoint Research 預估 ， 三星(Samsung) 憑藉 Galaxy Z 
Fold8 等大尺寸書本式設計的強勁動能 ， 預計在2026 年將以  38%
的市占率穩居全球龍頭寶座；而蘋果 (Apple) 推出的首款摺疊
iPhone ，雖預期在初期會面臨產能限制，但仍將為高階市場帶來
立即的衝擊，預估 2026 年就能迅速搶下 25% 的市占率空降全球
第二；而華為 (Huawei) 則預估以 22% 的市占率位居第三，主要由
於其在中國市場依然保持強勢的主導地位，並受到  Pura X Max 
等產品的推動。

叁、業 績 及 獲 利 預 估

一. 預估 2026 年稅後 EPS 63.08  
公司公告 9 月營收 16.13 億， MoM +5.6% ，YoY +32.2% ，3Q26
營收 45.02 億，QoQ +36.1% ，YoY +79.3% ，今年累計營收 124.3
億， YoY +47.3% 。展望 2H26 ，公司預期隨著 VR200 與 ASIC 伺
服器專案陸續放量，水冷快接頭與滑軌的出貨比重將顯著提升，
再加上新產能第四季將陸續開出，公司營收將維持成長動能。而
摺疊手機部分，受惠於客戶發布新款產品，亦將推升摺疊手機業
務回溫。此外，由於高毛利伺服器產品比重提升，公司預期毛利
率將持續攀升，且營業費用率亦將隨營收規模擴大而逐漸下降。
預估 2026 全年營收 181.8 億元， YoY +46.5% ，毛利率 35.2% ，
營利率 29.4% ，稅後淨利 43.2 億，全年稅後 EPS 63.08 元。

二. 預估 2027 年稅後 EPS 112.24 元 
展望 2027 年，受惠於 Vera Rubin 平台與 ASIC 進入主要放量階
段， QD 應用亦從 Switch Tray 延伸至 Compute Tray 及 Rack 
Manifold ， 將帶動機櫃快接頭用量提升 。 此外 ， 由於晶片功耗提升 、
冷卻液流量需求增加， NVIDIA 下一世代的 Vera Rubin Ultra 機
櫃，預期將導入更高規格 ZQD ，單價預估將比現行的 MQD/UQD
顯著提升，將有望帶動單櫃快接頭產值提升。預估 2027 全年營收
295.5 億元， YoY +46.5% ，毛利率 37.6% ，營利率 32.3% ，稅後
淨利 76.9 億，全年稅後 EPS 112.24 元。

9月经济数据快报
前三季度国内生产总值1071071亿元，按不变价格计算，同比增长4.6%。分产业看，第一产业
增加值58994亿元，同比增长3.7%；第二产业增加值383822亿元，增长3.8%；第三产业增加值
628255亿元，增长5.2%。分季度看，一季度国内生产总值同比增长5.0%，二季度增长4.3%，三
季度增长4.5%。从环比看，三季度国内生产总值增长1.2%。

前三季度，全国规模以上工业增加值同比增长5.3%。分三大门类看，采矿业增加值同比增长
2.0%，制造业增长5.7%，电力、热力、燃气及水生产和供应业增长5.4%。装备制造业增加值同比
增长10.2%，高技术制造业增加值增长14.3%，分别快于全部规模以上工业增加值4.9和9.0个百分
点。
前三季度，全国固定资产投资（不含农户）334115亿元，同比下降7.2%，与1—8月份持平；扣
除房地产开发的固定资产投资下降4.2%。其中，知识产权产品投资同比增长9.3%。高技术产业投
资同比增长4.9%。分产业看，第一产业投资同比下降3.9%，第二产业投资下降2.7%，第三产业投
资下降10.0%。
前三季度，货物进出口总额399399亿元，同比增长18.8%。其中，出口231979亿元，增长
16.4%；进口167420亿元，增长22.3%。对共建“一带一路”国家进出口增长17.1%。民营企业进
出口增长19.1%，占进出口总额的比重为56.9%。机电产品出口增长23.7%，占出口总额的比重为

Document 250: AI的饼越画越大，特斯拉为何仍难打动华尔街？

Source PDF: all report pdf/AI的饼越画越大，特斯拉为何仍难打动华尔街？.pdf
Report source: WallstreetCN (Global)
Date: 2026-10-22
Pages: 4
Status: ok

### Page 1

三⼤投⾏罕⻅地形成共识：进展真实存在，但估值已充分定价，甚⾄过于饱满。特斯拉正
⾯临⼀个愈发棘⼿的困境：故事越讲越⼤，估值却越来越难以⽀撑。
摘要：
特斯拉第三季度财报显⽰，公司⾃动驾驶、储能和机器⼈业务均取得阶段性进展，⽑利率亦超
出预期，但居⾼不下的运营⽀出、低于预期的盈利能⼒以及依然遥远的商业化兑现时间表，令
华尔街主流投⾏维持观望⽴场。这家市值 1.6 万亿美元的公司，正⾯临⼀个愈发棘⼿的困境：故
事越讲越⼤，估值却越来越难以⽀撑。
特斯拉第三季度营收 280 亿美元， EBIT 仅 7.18 亿美元，远低于⾼盛和摩根⼠丹利的预期，运营⽀
出超预期是主要拖累。尽管⾮ GAAP 每股摊薄收益 0.48 美元⼩幅超预期，但⾼盛已将 2026 ⾄
2027 年 EPS 预测下调，理由正是持续攀升的运营成本。对于股价⽽⾔，⾼盛维持中性评级及 360
美元⽬标价，较当前股价隐含约 7% 的下⾏空间；摩根⼠丹利同样维持中性评级，⽬标价 400 美
元；摩根⼤通对特斯拉股票标注中性评级。
三⼤投⾏罕⻅地形成共识：进展真实存在，但估值已充分定价，甚⾄过于饱满。

尽管如此，财报中的亮点同样不容忽视。北美 FSD 新⻋搭载率从⼆季度的 55% 升⾄超 75% ，⽆
⼈驾驶 Robotaxi 累计⾥程在短短数周内从 100 万英⾥增⾄ 310 万英⾥；储能业务⽑利率升⾄
33.1% ， Megapack 积压订单创历史纪录。这些数据为特斯拉的⻓期叙事提供了真实⽀撑，也是
多数分析师尚未彻底放弃这只股票的底层逻辑。
⽑利率惊喜，利润叙事受阻
特斯拉三季度整体⽑利率 19.6% ，全⾯超出各⽅预期 —— ⾼盛预测 18.5% ，公司汇总市场⼀致预
期 17.9% ， FactSet ⼝径为 17.5% 。
其中，汽⻋业务⽑利率（含股权激励，剔除碳积分）达 18.1% ，超出⾼盛预测的 16.3% ；储能业
务⽑利率 33.1% ，亦⼤幅超出摩根⼠丹利预测的 26.5% ；服务业务⽑利率 15.9% ，创历史新⾼。
⾸⻚ 资讯 快讯 ⾏情 ⽇历 APP VIP 会员 ⼤师课 ⻅闻历 搜索

### Page 2

然⽽，⽑利率层⾯的超预期被费⽤端的失控所抵消。三季度 EBIT 仅 7.18 亿美元，明显低于⾼盛
预测的 10.43 亿美元和摩根⼠丹利预测的 5.26 亿美元；研发费⽤ 27.61 亿美元，较摩根⼠丹利预
测⾼出约 11% 。管理层明确表⽰，为⽀撑 AI 与能源业务扩张 —— 包括 Optimus 、 Robotaxi 及
Megapack—— 运营⽀出和资本开⽀将继续按季环⽐增⻓。
⾼盛随即下调 2026 ⾄ 2027 年 EPS 预测，分别从 0.82 美元和 1.75 美元下修⾄ 0.67 美元和 1.60 美
元，同时略微上调 2028 年预测⾄ 2.50 美元。
摩根⼤通亦对此持审慎态度。按⽬前股价计算， 2028 年预期市盈率仍⾼达 154 倍，⾃由现⾦流收
益率在 2026 ⾄ 2027 年均为负值，这意味着投资者需要为遥远的未来⽀付极⾼的溢价。
Robotaxi 与 FSD ：⾥程碑在加速，但商业化仍在早期
Robotaxi 是本季度最受市场关注的业务进展。截⾄ 10 ⽉ 18 ⽇，特斯拉完全⽆⼈驾驶的 Robotaxi

累计⾏驶⾥程达 310 万英⾥，较 9 ⽉ 3 ⽇的约 100 万英⾥增⻓超过两倍，周增速约 20% ，超出⾼盛
此前预期的 200 ⾄ 300 万英⾥区间上限。
据⾼盛研究报告， 2026 年 1 ⽉ 22 ⽇特斯拉开始完全⽆监督运营⾄今，平均每约 10 万英⾥发⽣⼀
次事故（⽆论责任归属或严重程度）；相⽐之下， Waymo 在 2025 年 7 ⽉⾄ 2026 年 8 ⽉间平均每 18
万英⾥发⽣⼀次，近期数据已达每 36 万英⾥⼀次。差距依然存在，但特斯拉改善速度不可忽
视。
在地理扩张层⾯，管理层表⽰ Cybercab 已在奥斯汀展开运营，并预计下⽉在达拉斯启动；圣安
东尼奥和杰克逊维尔被列⼊后续扩张城市名单，此前已公布的凤凰城和拉斯维加斯亦在推进
中。
FSD 主动订阅⽤⼾数达 174 万，同⽐增⻓ 67% ；欧盟范围内的全⾯审批最早预计在 2026 年 12
⽉，管理层⽬标是在 2027 年⼀季度前获得欧盟统⼀许可。尽管进展积极，但⾼盛认为来⾃
Waymo 等竞争对⼿的压⼒不容低估，并将⾃动驾驶领域的竞争列为主要下⾏⻛险之⼀。

Optimus ：节奏⽐市场预期保守
⼈形机器⼈ Optimus 是本季度另⼀个被⾼度审视的议题。
⻢斯克在财报电话会上确认 Optimus 已在弗⾥蒙特⼯⼚开始量产，但对产能爬坡的表述相当审慎
——12 个⽉内⽬标产量为 1 万⾄ 2 万台，低于部分市场此前的乐观预期，这批机器⼈将⾸先⽤于
特斯拉内部的 Tesla Academy 进⾏⾃我训练和数据⽣成。⾯向外部客⼾的商业销售⽬标被推⾄
2027 年底，消费者端应⽤则更为遥远。
⻢斯克将 Optimus 的量产过程描述为 "S 曲线 " ，强调需要 " ⼀万件事同时运转正确 " ，速度最慢的零
部件或⼯艺将决定整体爬坡节奏。他点名⼿部（灵巧⼿）是当前最⼤的硬件瓶颈，实现⼈类级
别的灵活性和⻓时间可靠运作仍是挑战。
算⼒层⾯，特斯拉计划于 2027 年 4 ⽉将新⼀代 AI5 芯⽚集成进 Optimus ，该芯⽚算⼒据管理层介
绍约为现有 AI4 芯⽚的 10 倍，将⼤幅提升复杂⾏为能⼒。
据摩根⼤通研究报告，其对整个⼈形机器⼈供应链的⽴场因此趋于谨慎，并对 Unitree 、 Estun 、

UBTech 在未来 1 ⾄ 3 个⽉内⾯临预期修正⻛险保持警惕，。
储能与汽⻋需求：实质性亮点

在⼏项容易被 AI 叙事遮盖的核⼼业务中，储能和汽⻋需求实际上提供了令⼈信服的基本⾯⽀撑。
储能业务⽑利率 33.1% ，环⽐⼤幅提升；管理层称 Megapack 容量 "2027 年⼏乎已售罄 " ，能源业
务积压订单创历史纪录。德克萨斯州 Megafactory 已开始⽣产。
值得注意的是，三季度储能营收 28.81 亿美元低于⾼盛预测的 37.49 亿美元，管理层将部分差距
归因于对能源客⼾的关税退款；⾼盛表⽰将继续跟踪 ASP （平均售价）⾛势及定价环境。
汽⻋需求⽅⾯，管理层称乘⽤⻋积压订单处于四年最⾼⽔平，已在加拿⼤、澳⼤利亚、⽇本和
中国台湾实现创纪录交付， Tesla Semi 也开始量产。⾼盛将 2026 ⾄ 2028 年交付量预测上调⾄

184 万 /195 万 /203 万辆。供应链层⾯，电池可⽤性仍是全球产能爬坡的限制因素之⼀，但公司正
在柏林、德克萨斯和内华达多地推进相关产能建设。
估值 " 已充分定价 "
尽管业务进展不乏亮点，华尔街主流机构对特斯拉股票的态度依然⾼度⼀致。
⾼盛维持中性评级， 12 个⽉⽬标价 360 美元，基于 150 倍市盈率乘以未来 5 ⾄ 8 个季度 EPS 预测
（含股权激励），较当前股价约有 7% 的下⾏空间。摩根⼠丹利维持中性评级，⽬标价 400 美元，
其估值模型包含 44 美元的核⼼汽⻋业务、 145 美元的⽹络服务、 120 美元的 Tesla Mobility 、 35 美
元的储能，以及折扣后 56 美元的⼈形机器⼈价值分配，已相当充分地反映了⻓期期权价值。
⾼盛明确指出，当前估值 " 已充分定价 " （ valuation is full ），盈利能⼒和⾃由现⾦流在近⾄中期将
持续承压，⽽ EPS 改善的幅度和节奏将受到多个领域竞争压⼒的约束。特斯拉的 12 个⽉股价与

标普 500 指数相⽐，过去⼀年已跑输约 24% ，反映出市场在等待兑现的过程中持续消化溢价。
此外，⾼盛表⽰，特斯拉 CEO 在财报电话会上提及， SpaceX 于 10 ⽉ 20 ⽇的财报电话会上披露，
SpaceX 与特斯拉已分别成⽴由独⽴董事组成的特别委员会，就潜在业务合并进⾏讨论。这⼀披
露为特斯拉股票增添了新的变量，但当前信息有限，各⼤机构均表⽰将持续跟踪，尚未将其纳
⼊估值框架。

Document 284: Cliff Notes_ getting the balance right (2026-10-22)

Source PDF: all report pdf/Cliff Notes_ getting the balance right (2026-10-22).pdf
Report source: Westpac IQ
Date: 2026-10-22
Pages: 10
Status: ok

### Page 1

#cliffnotes #rba #australia #inflation #us #fomc #ecb #aud #usd
Cliff Notes: getting the balance right
By Ryan Wells, Illiana Jain & Elliot Clarke
10:46 October 16 2026 Share
Key insights from the week that was.
Australia’s key data release this week was the September Labour Force Survey. Employment surprised
to the downside, -39.3k. However, a 0.2ppt decline in labour force participation from August’s near-
historic high largely offset. The unemployment rate therefore held at 4.7% after August was revised up.

Residual seasonality may be in the mix. Regardless, the take home point from the update is that the

### Page 2

labour market is continuing to ease as employment growth runs below working-age population growth.
We expect the unemployment rate to grind higher into 2027 as tighter financial conditions and cost-of-
living pressures restrict activity growth to a below-trend pace. Although, our forecast peak is near at
4.8%, due by Q2 2027.
This update on the labour market complicates the communication challenge for the RBA. The
September minutes were firmly on the hawkish side, with the discussion focussing on materialising

upside risks to inflation. But, with the unemployment rate now through the RBA’s NAIRU assumption of
4.6%, Governor Bullock’s “a little tight” descriptor for the labour market is becoming harder to defend.
Still, with a clear focus on inflation risks – which also led us to revise up our near-term CPI forecasts in
the October Market Outlook – we still expect a 25bp rate hike at the November meeting.
Over in the US, the headline CPI rose 0.6% as expected in September, taking the annual rate up to

3.7%yr. Energy prices were a key contributor (5.2%, 20.4%yr), while core inflation was a touch above
consensus in the month at 0.3% (2.6%yr). Within the core components, core goods prices gained 0.3%
after being broadly unchanged over the past 6 months (0.7%yr). Core services meanwhile rose 0.4%
(3.1%yr) on a 0.4% increase in shelter (3.2%yr), a 0.6% gain for transport services and a 0.3%
increase in medical care services (2.7%yr and 2.5%yr respectively). Further, the subsequent release of

the PPI pointed to continued upstream price pressures, with the headline and core measures up
6.3%yr and 5.0%yr respectively. The latest Beige Book provided colour on these developments, firms
across multiple districts citing tariffs, insurance and commodity prices as ongoing sources of cost
pressure. Guiding on the risks, many respondents expect further price increases as contracts/hedges
roll off.
Given their telegraphed concerns over inflation, there is enough signal in these reports to warrant a

follow-up hike at the October FOMC meeting. Though it seems Committee members believe they are
near the end of the tightening cycle and so have time on their side. This week, Daly and Bowman both
showed patience in assessing the trend, with no clear bias towards an October or December decision.
Williams stated he believes one more hike would prove sufficient in this cycle given known information.
And Waller also showed confidence, highlighting that elevated long-term yields are doing some of the

FOMC’s work, restricting demand in interest rate sensitive sectors. We hold a similar view, anticipating
that one more hike by year end and a little time will bring inflation near the 2.0%yr medium-term target.
However, as discussed in this month’s Market Outlook, capacity pressures are likely to see inflation
consistently above target, rather than below, in coming years – particularly if, as we suspect, capacity
in the housing sector remains tight. This will mean the FOMC have to remain modestly restrictive over

the forecast horizon and will also bias up long-term yields.
In Asia, there was one release that stood out, China’s trade account. At USD133.5bn, the September
result was a whisker inside January 2025’s record high of USD137.9bn. More impressively, throughout
2025 and 2026 year-to-date the surplus has now averaged USD100.9bn, almost 3 times the average of
2019, prior to the pandemic. The geographic breadth of export growth is as impressive as the headline

annual gain of 36.3%yr, even more so given the ongoing strength in Chinese investment in
neighbouring nations’ production chains. In time, both trade and income receipts from offshore
investment should buoy the renminbi, although for now continued outbound foreign investment should
moderate the pace of gains against other nations in the region, and likely versus the US dollar.

### Page 3

Related articles
Another key trend assessed in October’s Market Outlook is the evolution of producer prices across
Asia, and the likelihood of the disinflationary benefit the west has received in recent decades
continuing. Historically, labour cost arbitrage was one of the key drivers of declining goods prices.
While still possible, we expect the next phase of manufacturing development to instead be driven by
capital, technology and energy. Many end users and the manufacturers undertaking production are

also increasingly prioritising resilience, security and specialisation over cost minimisation. It is therefore
more probably future supply-chain developments will help contain rising costs through productivity than
drive sustained price declines for the goods bought by consumers and businesses.

EUROZONE: IMPROVING BUSINESS SENTIMENT, DRIVEN BY TECH, IS SPREADING TO OTHER SECTORS
Stéphane Colliac & Tarik Rharrab
*LATEST OBSERVATION: 5 OCTOBER 2026  **ESTIMATE
The September PMI surveys confirm that growth in the Eurozone is getting stronger, boosted in particular by new export orders, which are
still rising. Sectors that were already performing well – technology and capital goods (including for defence) – are continuing to grow, while

those that had been struggling until now – aeronautics, consumer goods and financial services – are seeing their production indices recover.
This strong performance is particularly noteworthy, given that it has not yet been undermined by higher inflation.
The heatmap shows the PMI index readings: a reading above 50 indicates expansion, while a reading below 50 signals contraction compared to the previous
month. For each indicator, sectors are ranked in descending order based on their September 2026 reading.

Shades of red represent values above 50, while shades of blue represent values below 50: the intensity of the colour increases the further away they are
from this threshold.
Production growth spreads to more sectors
The PMI surveys on business sentiment confirm that business activity in the Eurozone is expanding at a faster pace and spreading to more sectors.
Household-consumption-related sectors (goods and services), which were adversely affected in the spring by the outbreak of the conflict in the Middle

East, do not appear to be negatively impacted by the rising inflation observed since August. The aeronautics sector, where production has mainly been
limited by supply constraints, is also showing signs of improvement.

These trends, although still moderate, come alongside a drive to upgrade equipment, fuelled by investment in digital technology, electrification
(which is supporting the automotive sector) and defence. The momentum in technology goods and services further strengthened in September.
The production index for computer and electronics hit a five-year high, while the software services production index stayed close to its July peak.

Financial services are also enjoying a significant improvement, reflecting the healthy lending momentum despite interest rates starting to rise
(see our analysis).
Growth driven by exports
Exports drove growth in the first half of 2026, both across the Eurozone as a whole (see our analysis) and among its key members (notably
Germany). This momentum is also increasing, as indicated by the ‘new export orders’ component of the various PMI indices. As was the case with

production, there has been a particularly noteworthy increase for capital goods (technology and machinery) and related services, particularly digital
services. This trend is playing out more broadly, as, in September, new export orders rose across all sub-sectors, with the exception of intermediate
goods and consumer goods, where contraction recently eased. There has been a significant improvement in the transport sector, particularly in
the aeronautics industry, which has major markets in Asia. Recent data on French exports show that aeronautics exports to this region are rising

once again.
The convergence between rising production and increasing export-order backlogs shows that Eurozone growth is based on an increasingly solid
foundation. Inflationary pressures are not holding it back for the time being.
stephane.colliac@bnpparibas.com
tarik.rharrab@bnpparibas.com
With the help of Ece Usta (intern).

Document 324: Five ways the UK’s Autumn Budget could shock us

Source PDF: all report pdf/Five ways the UK’s Autumn Budget could shock us.pdf
Report source: ING Think
Date: 2026-10-16
Pages: 10
Status: ok

### Page 1

From better ﬁscal headroom to surprise income tax hikes, this
is how UK Chancellor John Healey's ﬁrst budget could surprise
investors
James Smith
UK Chancellor John Healey
In a nutshell: What we expect
Higher debt interest costs look set to drive the level of 'ﬁscal
headroom' from £24bn in March to £10bn. There's a growing
sense that Chancellor John Healey would like to get this up
to £15bn or higher. The government is set to modestly
increase spending on the cost of living, though the total

intervention is unlikely to meaningfully top £5bn and it's
likely to be mostly billed as temporary to avoid counting
against the main ﬁscal rule. We expect an increase in capital
spending of roughly £10bn/year, bearing in mind this is
exempt from the ﬁscal rule dictating that the current budget
must be in surplus after ﬁve years. To rebuild headroom

### Page 2

against that rule, we expect £5-6bn of tax hikes, likely via a
higher Bank Surcharge and possibly broadening the National
Insurance tax base.
In short, we don't expect the budget to deliver a material
stimulus (or add materially to the already-planned ﬁscal
consolidation). The impact on the Bank of England, which is
poised to hike rates in November, is likely to be fairly
minimal.
But all chancellors like to keep a rabbit in their hat – and what
follows are ﬁve ways it could surprise us.

1. Fiscal headroom goes up – not down
What if the UK’s ﬁscal hole isn’t nearly as bad as we think?
The widespread expectation going into the UK’s Autumn Budget on
28 October is that the Chancellor is going to have to face up to
higher debt interest costs with some modest tax hikes.
Bank of England rate expectations are almost a percentage point
higher than they were at the time of the Oﬃce for Budget
Responsibility’s March assessment of Britain’s public ﬁnances. 10-

year bond yields are some 70 basis points higher, which, taken
together, will push up debt interest spending by £11bn/year by
2029/30.
That points to a sizeable reduction in the amount of money the
government has left under its main ﬁscal rule, which requires the
current budget (day-to-day spending, including debt interest less
revenue) to be in surplus within three years. Back in March, that
surplus, or ﬁscal headroom, was projected at £24bn in 2029/30.

Debt interest costs are rising
Cookie settings

### Page 3

*Based on the average rate in the 10 days up to 25 September, which
likely represents the OBR's observation window for this budget
Source: Macrobond, ING
We think that headroom will have dropped to around £10bn before
any corrective action. And the vibe from the Treasury suggests the
Chancellor would prefer it to be above £15bn, to give more
breathing space in case of future adverse forecast revisions. That
would point to some limited tax hikes.
But what if that’s all wrong? The truth is, nobody really knows how

the OBR will change its assessment of the UK economy. And as we
saw last year, the Treasury is often inclined to talk up the doom and
gloom to open up a positive surprise on the day. 12 months ago,
the hit to headroom was considerably smaller than had been
widely expected.
This time, any positive surprise is most likely to come from wage
growth. Will the rise in energy prices spill into higher pay, just as it
did in 2022? We think it won’t, not least because the jobs market is

much weaker, an assessment we suspect the OBR will share.
But even relatively small upgrades to wage growth can make a big
diﬀerence to the government’s fortunes. Since 2010, the OBR has
revised the level of earnings across its ﬁve-year forecast by an
average of 1.3 percentage points in absolute terms. If it did that
now, say by lifting wage growth over the next two years by 0.6ppt
apiece, it would add £10bn to headroom, almost perfectly
oﬀsetting the hit from higher interest costs.

### Page 4

There are also reasons to think the impact of lower migration
projections, which some reports suggest could wipe another £7bn
oﬀ headroom via lower tax revenue forecasts, could be much less
than feared. The starting point for employment looks a little higher
than it did back in March. The rise in global equities this year should
also be helpful for capital gains tax projections.
Why does this all matter? Because the higher the headroom, the
less ‘need’ there is to hike taxes, and if it is signiﬁcantly higher than

expected, the more room there is to increase spending under the
ﬁscal rules.
The simple message is this: don’t judge the Autumn Budget by the
level of headroom left over.
2. Spending splurge
The problem with ‘headroom’ is not only that it’s highly sensitive to
the OBR’s economic outlook, but that the forward-looking nature of
the ﬁscal rules opens the door to a lot of creative accounting.
The impact of higher inﬂation is a case in point. This helps the

Treasury through higher receipts – both VAT and income tax. Yes, it
also pushes up the cost of indexed beneﬁts. But importantly,
departmental budgets don’t automatically increase to oﬀset the
impact of higher prices.
That opens up a classic trick from the Treasury, which is to leave
spending plans virtually unchanged in nominal terms, setting up
substantially smaller real-terms budget increases across the
medium term.
Even before the Iran War, government plans pencilled in real-terms

cuts to per capita departmental spending in FY2029. Given some
functions, like health, are ‘protected’, that implies much deeper cuts
to smaller departments.
Suﬃce it to say, that is unrealistic. Higher inﬂation will push up the
wage bill across government. It would cost £12bn to simply
maintain those March departmental spending plans in real terms.
That’s before considering the cost of ending that austerity pencilled

### Page 5

in for later this decade, or how the government will fund its defence
or social care ambitions.
A top-up to government departments is inevitable – the question is
when. It looks like the Treasury wants to kick the can on these tricky
budget decisions until next autumn, when it plans to release its
next Spending Review, a process of allocating money across
departments. No doubt the hope is that debt interest costs will
have fallen and the economic outlook brightened, such that there's

more space under the ﬁscal rules to make bigger spending
commitments.
That points to a very modest package of spending measures this
month, mainly targeting the cost of living. On top of the already-
announced cut to VAT on energy, we’d imagine the Treasury will
replicate last year’s budget and lower household bills by funding a
greater share of ‘policy costs’ from general taxation. This has the
added beneﬁt of lowering headline inﬂation at a particularly

sensitive moment for the Bank of England. Crucially though, the
government is likely to bill these moves as temporary, so they don’t
count under that main ﬁscal rule.
But if the economic picture really is better than feared, don’t rule
out the government going further. A decision to fully fund the UK’s
3% defence spending commitment would be a major wildcard,
though this would likely come at the expense of capital plans in
other areas of government.

Ultimately, the key thing to watch is the OBR’s assessment of how
all these policy changes aﬀect GDP growth and borrowing over the
next year. If there’s a material stimulus eﬀect, it could add to
market bets on UK rate hikes.
3. A manifesto break on income tax
Tax hikes are likely at the Autumn Budget, but should be very small.
We’re probably looking at a package of circa £5bn (0.2% of GDP).
Part of the problem is that the list of viable options looks perilously

small if Labour is to avoid breaking its manifesto pledge not to raise

the major taxes, which together account for around 70% of total

Our best guess is that the budget will include a hike in the Bank
Surcharge (raising £2bn if increased from 3% to 8%), together with
a broadening of the tax base for National Insurance to landlords
and partners in LLPs (together yielding around £2bn/year). Other
options, like ending a loophole on stamp duty for commercial
property or lowering the VAT threshold for businesses, have also

been on the table.
The problem with many of these measures is that they require
massive changes to stand any chance of raising material sums,
risking big behavioural reactions while oﬀering uncertain revenue
potential. There’s a reason why former chancellor Rachel Reeves
shied away from many of the measures we outline in the chart
below.
How the Chancellor could raise taxes

Is there scope for a surprise? We think there are two main ways on
tax – one good, one bad.
The good? A surprise announcement of a so-called ‘tax switch’, a
novel proposal from Resolution Foundation which would see the
government simultaneously raise basic income tax and cut

employee National Insurance by 2ppt. The impact on workers
would be neutral, but because income tax has a broader base
(including pensions), this would raise £6bn/year.
Though not a substantial sum, it would signal to investors that
Prime Minister Andy Burnham isn’t afraid to go near the thorny
issue of raising taxes. But in light of recent pension changes,

### Page 8

including to the infamous ‘triple lock’, this feels like too much of a
political gamble at this stage.
The negative surprise would be if the Treasury decided to raise the
basic tax allowance. Burnham ﬂirted with this on his ﬁrst day in
oﬃce; it would not only be expensive (potentially £10bn), but it
would also signal that the UK’s commitment to the threshold freeze
– which is doing the lion's share of the planned ﬁscal consolidation
– is waning.
A big rise in the National Living Wage

The minimum wage increase in April 2027, which will be
announced alongside the budget, is perhaps the most important
variable for the Bank of England.
This is based on a recommendation from the Low Pay Commission,
which earlier this year provisionally estimated the increase at 3.7%.
That’s a smaller increase than over the past two years and,
crucially, is not a million miles above the level the Bank considers
consistent with a 2% medium-term inﬂation target (3.25%).

But the LPC has some ﬂexibility in its recommendation, and one
criterion is the cost of living. Higher inﬂation means we could see a
ﬁnal proposal between 4% and 4.5%. Anything above that would,
we suspect, be problematic for the Bank of England and would
bolster the case for higher rates.
The government is no doubt well aware of this. And there appears
to be growing recognition among Labour ﬁgures, including on the
left of the party, that the big minimum wage increases, coupled

with previous payroll tax increases, have had a big impact on
business – particularly in consumer services.
But if the LPC were to recommend minimum wage growth of close
to 5%, could the government resist it? In theory, yes. But politically,
we suspect not.
A surge in bond issuance
We already know that bond issuance is set to increase again in
2027, though for reasons unrelated to the UK’s ﬁscal trajectory.

Still, the point is that, beneath these numbers, public sector net
borrowing is still likely to be projected to keep falling through the
rest of the decade. The major risk – related to some of the things

discussed earlier, like larger near-term spending or surprise
increases in tax allowances – would jeopardise this fundamental
improvement in bond issuance in the medium term. That's
something investors wouldn’t take kindly to.

Document 380: Sales Trader Commentary_ TAIEX 49k Wobble - July De Ja Vu, or Just Pausing Before the Santa Rally_ _ GSTW

Source PDF: all report pdf/Sales Trader Commentary_ TAIEX 49k Wobble - July De Ja Vu, or Just Pausing Before the Santa Rally_ _ GSTW.pdf
Report source: line報告備份
Date: 2026-10-13
Pages: 8
Status: ok

### Page 1

Sales Trader Commentary: TAIEX 49k Wobble -
July De Ja Vu, or Just Pausing Before the Santa
Rally? | GSTW
13 October 2026 | 4:11 PM Taiwan Standard Time
Robert Wang
Goldman Sachs (Asia) L.L.C. Taipei Branch
FICC & Equities
Index lost the 49k points threshold and saw the biggest decline on a point basis since
the end of July. Market seemed to lose the steam to break through 50k points after
the past choppy sessions. Is this July de ja vu or some healthy pullback? Foreigners

and domestic investors seem to share different narrative. Check out below for our
investor feedback.
Key Investor Feedback and Observations
Positioning. we saw meaningful outflows today from all kinds of investors today,
again focusing in ex-TSMC large caps. Today foreigners net sold US$5.2bn today,
the second largest in the history (simultaneously drove TWD weakness over 32.23
today). Domestic investors, especially retail, on the other hand, anchored the

market with the record high margin loan balance (as of 10/12/2026). Local funds
remained buyer as well mainly thanks to ETFs inflow.
Macro. CPI headline brought uncertainty and forced global investors to turn
cautious, which was exacerbated by high yields and crude. For Taiwan, AI trade is
relatively resilient to the Middle Eastern tension and high energy prices. However,
at some point, it is going to have a real economic impact either through Fed or

some other macro data.
Sentiment. Surprisingly, people are still bullish in Taiwan, though they also
acknowledge some choppy trading sessions ahead. Local investor feedback is not
calling for capitulation; in fact, most believe this is just another "two-steps forward
one-step backward" cadence towards another record high. Nonetheless, some
retail start taking chips off the table. This echoes our observation among the
foreign investors. Yes, the AI fundamental future is all sunshine, but as we head into

the end of year, it is natural for them to protect their great fun thus far this year
instead of adding on immediate risk. I'd imagine the next few weeks are going to be
full of 1) rapid sector rotation; 2) weak or unsustainable momentum before Santa
comes into town after the midterm election for our year-end rally Christmas gift.
TSMC Earnings and Others. We know the numbers are going to be good, and we
also know people are going to sell-on-facts after TSMC earnings call; recall most

recent one happened right after the meeting that scarred a lot of investors since.
Perhaps this time people started trimming ahead (and maybe too early?), which
provides some window for a quick rebound into small and mid cap. OCP summit is
happening right now, and TPCA Show will start next week. Watch out for single
stock movement mentioned in the conferences.

Sector. 1) ABF/CCL is still the crowded long sectors for both foreign and domestic
investor, but echoing on above points, investors need more catalysts to add on
risks or drive the re-ratings. 2) Power is another big issues that data centers need
to resolve, power solutions and fuel cells name are also key focus in Taiwan. 3) Let's
not forget about OSATs. We need more equipment and testing capacity to suffice
enlarging demand from both ASIC and GPU.

Key Stocks I Follow: Unimicron, Kinsus, NYPCB, ZDT, EMC, TUC, Delta, Lite-On,
Kaori, ASE, KYEC, All Ring
Risk. Upside looks thin in the short term. Good earnings are not enough - look at

would describe the past few days, but maybe it's time to find some value Buy?

performance) of their underlying index or benchmark during the same period of time. This effect can be magnified in volatile markets.

Document 381: Salesforce 57亿美元买下Braze：58%溢价背后，营销云补短板的关键一役

Source PDF: all report pdf/Salesforce 57亿美元买下Braze：58%溢价背后，营销云补短板的关键一役.pdf
Report source: Unknown
Date: 未知日期
Pages: 2
Status: ok

### Page 1

Salesforce 以近 57 亿美元企业价值、每股 47.50 美元收购 AI 营销平台 Braze ，预计 2028 财
年完成。此举意在补⻬增⻓乏⼒的 Marketing Cloud 短板，吸收受企业客⼾⻘睐的竞争对
⼿，并与 Agentforce 整合加速 AI 客⼾互动落地。交易⽆需债务融资，但⾼溢价下整合与收
⼊转化成效待检验。
摘要：
Salesforce 重启⼤规模并购，以近 57 亿美元企业价值收购⼈⼯智能营销平台 Braze ，试图补⻬营
销云业务的竞争短板，并加速 AI 智能体在客⼾互动领域的商业化落地。
周⼆，据彭博报道， Salesforce 宣布，将以每股 47.50 美元收购 Braze 全部股份，较后者周⼀收
盘价溢价 58% 。交易预计于 Salesforce 2028 财年内完成，即 2027 年 2 ⽉⾄ 2028 年 1 ⽉期间。
Salesforce ⾸席运营兼财务官 Robin Washington 在周⼆的分析师电话会议上表⽰， Braze 将增强

公司⾯向⾼流量 B2C 品牌的实时个性化移动互动能⼒，同时带来⼯程和销售⼈才。
收购完成后， Braze 将与 Salesforce 旗舰 AI ⼯具 Agentforce 深度整合，重点服务营销及客⼾沟通
业务。
营销云增⻓承压， Salesforce ⾼溢价补短板
Salesforce 营销业务近⼏个季度增⻓乏⼒， Marketing Cloud 的表现尤其承压。 Piper Sandler 分
析师 Billy Fitzsimmons 指出， Marketing Cloud 增速放缓，部分原因在于 Braze 持续抢占市场份
额。此次收购将使 Salesforce 直接吸收这⼀受到企业客⼾⻘睐的竞争对⼿，减少⾃⾝营销产品
线的竞争压⼒。
总部位于纽约的 Braze 专注于实时客⼾互动，通过采集客⼾数据，借助 AI 在移动应⽤、⽹站、电
⼦邮件和短信等渠道⽣成个性化内容。⽬前，该公司服务约 2770 家企业客⼾，包括 Canva 、
Equinox 、 Instacart 和 Venmo 等。

对 Salesforce ⽽⾔，这笔交易不只是扩⼤产品版图，更关乎能否及时补⻬ AI 营销能⼒。公司⾼管
表⽰， Marketing Cloud 新版本已引⼊智能体 AI 功能，客⼾正逐步迁移⾄新平台。 Braze 在实时
互动和个性化营销⽅⾯的技术与客⼾资源，有望加快这⼀转型进程。
此次收购也牵动整个营销软件板块。消息公布后， Klaviyo 和 HubSpot 股价周⼆早盘明显上涨。
彭博智库分析师 Niraj Patel 和 Maria Beltran 指出，这些企业正直接⾯临 OpenAI 、 Anthropic 等 AI
⼒量带来的冲击。 Salesforce 愿意以 58% 的溢价收购 Braze ，也令市场重新审视传统营销软件企
业在 AI 时代的竞争壁垒与估值。
重启⼤规模并购，整合效益将接受检验
此次交易标志着 Salesforce 在经历⼀段并购放缓期后，再度加⼤外延扩张⼒度。 2023 年初，在
激进投资者施压后，公司暂停⼤规模并购；此后， Salesforce 重新启动收购，先后以 36 亿美元收

购 Fin 、以 15 亿美元收购 Contentful ，并以 80 亿美元完成对 Informatica 的收购。
财务层⾯， Washington 表⽰，此次交易⽆需债务融资。交割后， Salesforce 将推进业务整合，
以提升运营效率。Braze 上⼀财年⽑利率约为 69% ，低于 Salesforce 约 78% 的⽔平，双⽅在成本
结构和运营效率⽅⾯存在⼀定的整合空间。

Report Introduction
Our proprietary dbDataInsights survey shows cyber threats are evolving faster than organisations can adapt
Source: dbDataInsights survey of cybersecurity experts, September 2026; Cybersecurity Survey 2026, Deutsche Bank Research.
“It’s the only thing that can shut down my business.”
That was the punchline from a conversation I had recently with the CEO of a large
privately held financial services company. This was his comment when I followed up

as to why cybersecurity risk is the number one concern that “keeps him up at night”.
To help understand this risk, our dbDataInsights team surveyed more than 50
cybersecurity experts. The uncomfortable conclusion is that many firms are
unprepared for cyber threats. Cyber risk is increasing as AI adoption accelerates.
—89% agree the threat landscape is evolving faster than organisations can adapt.
—78% say the gap between highly and poorly prepared organisations is widening.

What has changed with AI advancement? Cyberattacks have become faster,
cheaper, more deceptive and more scalable. Capabilities once reserved for
sophisticated actors are now broadly accessible to a new generation of profit-driven
criminals. At the same time, rising geopolitical tension has made cyber operations
an increasingly attractive low-cost, deniable tool for nation states. Corporates and
investors therefore need to understand their broader exposure and critical

infrastructure risk in their supply chain and with stakeholders.
Among other autonomous incidents, the recent Hugging Face incident
highlighted AI’s cyberattack potential. AI agents caused harm without
direction from a human attacker, with weak guardrails and no human
judgement to restrain them. Beyond the systemic questions it raised, the attack
highlights three additional concerns: (i) AI operates at a speed and scale humans
cannot match, with agents taking over 17,000+ actions in four days; (ii) the intrusion

began in the data-processing pipeline, where AI platforms are uniquely exposed;
and (iii) the agents escaped their test environment through a widely used third-party
tool representing supplier risk in an unfamiliar form.

### Page 4

Report summary and notable takeaways
Secular trends and AI adoption have widened the attack surface; the severity of losses is compounding
Source: FBI Internet Crime Complaint Center (IC3), company security advisories, OECD, company reports, Deutsche Bank Research.
Secular trends have widened the attack surface. This includes rapid
digitalisation, widespread Internet of Things (IoT) endpoints, hybrid workforces,
cloud adoption and deeper supply chain dependencies. The scale of the threat

increase is reflected in data from the FBI Internet Crime Complaint Center (IC3),
which shows cybercrime complaints more than doubled from 2019 pre-pandemic
baseline of ~467,000 to ~1.0mn in 2025, with a 3x expansion relative to 2017 levels.
Barriers to entry are lower; attack tools are now sold “off the shelf”. Today,
cybercrime marketplaces, available on the dark web, look less like the underworld
portrayed in films and more like an online software store. Buyers can access AI-

powered tools that scan for software vulnerabilities (see top-right chart showing
reported CVEs after the release of cyber capable frontier models), write malicious
code at speed, and provide access to malware that rewrites its own code to avoid
detection as it runs. Ransomware-as-a-service (RaaS) takes it further, renting out
ready-made attack kits, often in exchange for a portion of the profits.
Impersonation is becoming harder to detect. Hackers can now generate more

realistic phishing messages, while voice cloning and deepfakes make impersonation
convincing enough to gain access unnoticed. With stolen credentials, attackers often
aren’t hacking in, they are simply logging in.
The rise of loose cybercriminal collectives. Some of the most damaging recent
attacks have been linked to loosely organised, often English-speaking networks such
as ShinyHunters and Scattered Spider. The latter has been widely named as the

group behind the 2025 Marks & Spencer cyberattack, in which the hacking group
deployed RaaS. AI is enabling these groups to become larger and faster, widening
the gap between the speed of attack and a defender’s response.
The severity of losses is compounding. A 2026 IBM study puts the average breach
at a record USD ~5.0mn, up 12% YoY, but the impact on large companies can run
into hundreds of millions or even billions. Estimates for global cyber insurance

premiums (per Munich Re) point to a rise from roughly USD 7.0bn in 2020 to USD
15.0bn in 2025, with expectations to reach USD 28.0bn by 2030.

### Page 5

5
Among the more concerning survey takeaways is that a large-scale and
significant systemic incident is now the base case.
— 83% expect a significant systemic attack within two years and 81% expect it to be
moderately, very, or extremely severe for economic activity and markets.
— 81% say AI favours attackers today, with not one respondent putting defenders
ahead of attackers. Only 20% expect defenders to be ahead in five years.
Geopolitical tensions coupled with rising cyber capabilities have transformed

cyberspace into a digital battlefield between global powers. Intent has moved
towards espionage and disruption of not only critical infrastructure (e.g., healthcare,
finance, power, industrial systems, defence) but private companies as well. Chinese
President Xi Jinping has linked cybersecurity to national security and economic and
social stability, with China’s public statements similarly emphasising the protection
of critical infrastructure. At the same time, the US’s June 2025 Executive Order on

Cybersecurity reflects its continued focus on national cyber resilience.
Europe relies on US cyber tools while its start-ups lag in funding. Reliance on US
security vendors exposes European firms to potential access, update or export
restrictions over the long-term. Shrinking this gap will prove difficult given US cyber
start-ups attract nearly 6x the venture and growth funding of European peers. And
while the timing of quantum computing (which could break widely used encryption

methods) remains uncertain, it could further deepen Europe’s dependency.
Ultimately, cyber preparedness is a financial variable. Investors should ask what
firms spend on security versus AI adoption, how well they know their vulnerabilities,
and how fast they can recover.
— Only 24% of respondents think organisations have sufficient visibility into the
threats relevant to them, even though 57% saw cybersecurity budgets rise over
the past 12 months.

— 81% agree that cybersecurity related laws and regulation cannot keep pace, and
65% doubt leadership understands cyber risk well enough.

### Page 6

6
When cyber weakness = share price consequence…
Investors are more aware of cyber risk today, but they rarely sell a stock due to
perceived cyber vulnerabilities (although we are aware of some who have).
—What is the potential impact? Our dbDataInsights event study of 47 breaches
shows a cumulative breach-attributed return of c. -2.9% within the first two
trading days and c. -6.0% through first 20 days trading days. The erosion then
continues more gradually, reaching c. -6.7% nine months after disclosure (see

slide 13).
Which industries are most vulnerable?
—Our survey respondents point to (1) government & public sector (52%), (2)
financial services (50%), (3) energy and utilities (46%) and (4) healthcare and life
sciences follow (39%, rising to 48% among US respondents).
We should caveat, that high sector exposure does not mean every company in it is
equally vulnerable. Some firms in these “exposed” sectors may very well be better
prepared given they have built specialist teams and defence protocols for decades.

What about access to credit and ratings? Credit and rating providers are paying
attention. Moody’s and S&P both cut their outlook on Jaguar Land Rover within two
months of its 2025 hack. Today, this type of impact is not widespread. But if risk
escalates through a systemic incident or an AI-driven step change in attacks these
mechanisms, including ESG ratings, could reprice asset ownership or impact access
to credit. In fact, some lenders are already pricing it. Journal of Corporate Finance

research using US syndicated loan data found borrowers with persistently higher cyber
risk pay 4–13bp more.
The bottom line? While a broader systemic attack is hard to prepare for, corporates
and investors should be asking questions about vulnerabilities, security investment,
preparedness and recovery should an attack occur. This is especially true for
industries quickly adopting AI capabilities and / or a high threat landscape.

dbDataInsights Cybersecurity Expert Survey methodology
—This survey was conducted among 54 cybersecurity professionals across the US
and Europe in September of 2026. Questions took the form of either multiple

choice selections or free form responses. The dbDataInsights team received the
responses anonymously, but respondents were required to work or consult in
cyber security roles
—Who took the survey? On self-identified roles, around 80% sit inside an
organisation’s cybersecurity function, with the remainder working independently
(15%) or in academia and research (6%). The most common titles were security
architect (23%), Chief Information Security Officer (16%) and security analyst (9%),

alongside Head of Security, Deputy CISO, Incident Response Lead and Security
Operations Lead.
—Only a small minority of respondents work for cyber resellers, thus survey views
are primarily attributable to those working within an organization on
cybersecurity.
—Survey participants were also asked for their perspectives on cyber resilience and
governance, what is misunderstood, alongside emerging threats, risk to AI
adoption and the influence of geopolitics.

—While responses were anonymous, in some cases we conducted follow up
interviews with certain participants where contact details were made available.
Source: dbDataInsights survey of cybersecurity experts, September 2026; Cybersecurity Survey 2026, Deutsche Bank Research.
